#3607. The Fitbit Edge Is Everything the Charge 6 Should Have Been

I’ve been using the new Fitbit Edge for a little over a week, and am happy to report that it’s quite good at its job. It shipped with more features than I expected (Google Maps directions were there from the jump) and corrects the missteps that Google made with the Fitbit Charge 6. After running the Fitbit Edge through its paces, here’s my breakdown of what works well, what doesn’t, and what I think of the device overall.

When I reviewed it in May 2025, I liked a lot about the Charge 6, but found it had a few serious flaws. Tightening the strap messed up its GPS readings (weird but true), and some of its features required a subscription (like the music controls that only worked with YouTube Music). Its promised side button wasn’t really a button, and didn’t even always work.

I’m happy to report that all of my biggest gripes have been fixed in the Fitbit Edge. Or to put it another way: the Edge is what the Charge should have been in the first place.

Accuracy tests are always one of my top priorities in testing, but honestly I wasn’t expecting much from this device. A Fitbit can still be successful in my eyes even if its heart rate sensor is only “good enough.” Nobody should expect lab-level precision from a Fitbit.

But I was pleasantly surprised by just how capable the Edge is in this regard. It sometimes takes a few minutes to lock on to a heart rate signal (as most optical devices do), but after it does, it is spot-on—I think it might be in the running alongside the Apple Watch and high-end Garmins as one of the better heart rate wearables on the market. The Edge’s accuracy is notably better than the Fitbit Air's, even though they seem to share the same sensor hardware.

Location accuracy is also quite good, only deviating slightly from a Garmin I tested alongside the Edge. It may not be the best out there, but it’s miles ahead of what the Charge 6 was able to do.

The Fitbit Edge has real music controls, unlike the Charge 6. I can play something in Spotify or in Castro (my favorite podcast app) on my phone, and then control the audio from my wrist—and you no longer need to use YouTube Music or have a subscription to get this feature.

When the Fitbit Air and the Google Health app first launched, it was possible to pair both a Fitbit and a Pixel Watch to the same phone, but not two Fitbits. That has finally changed: I was able to connect the Edge to my phone without losing my connection to the Air. It also works to swap seamlessly between the two Fitbits. For example, one night the Edge was low on battery, so I slept with the Air on, and then swapped back in the morning once the Edge was charged. The app happily showed my sleep data from the Air and my activity data from the Edge without my having to do mess around in the app.

As a nice bonus, the charger for the Fitbit Edge is the same as for the Fitbit Air. (The chargers for the Pixel Watch and for the Fitbit Charge 6 are not compatible with the Edge.)

The Fitbit Edge is a similar size as the old Charge 6, but with noticeably more screen real estate. The strap fits pretty much the same way as a Pixel watch band—that is to say, normally, not with the strange hang-off-the-wrist stiffness of the Charge 6.

The Fitbit Edge also has a physical button—not a haptic nub like the Charge 6, but an actual, physical button. And it even works (to return you to the home screen) nearly every time I press it. The Charge 6’s nub was not as reliable.

Overall, I’m quite happy with the Fitbit Edge. It’s much better than the Charge 6, and adds accuracy and a screen to the things I already liked about the Fitbit Air. But there are a few things I don’t totally love.

One is that there is no NFC chipset, which means no option to use Google Pay. I don’t mind using my phone or card at checkouts (and I always carry my phone), so this isn’t a big deal for me, but I know a lot of folks love a wrist-based payment, and that’s missing from the Fitbit Edge. (Google reportedly said most Fitbit users didn’t actually use Google Pay, so they removed the functionality—presumably to fit in the other components while keeping the device small.)

The other downside is, I hate to say it, the Google Health app. It’s OK, but I still find myself arguing with the AI when it assigns me workouts I never asked for, and I have to agree with the redditors who are commiserating about the lack of a “yesterday” view. (On the bright side, Google recently added a feature that lets you turn off the AI insights, which helps a lot.)

Overall, the Fitbit Edge is a great fitness tracker. It measures heart rate and running/walking mileage accurately; it can control music in a sensible way; and it looks good, feels good to wear, and works smoothly. The price also tracks: It’s definitely worth the $20 premium over the Charge 6, and it’s not quite double the price of the Air, which I think is fair. For anyone who doesn’t want a smartwatch or a running watch and will be satisfied with just “a Fitbit,” this one is pretty much perfect.

#3606. Julian Alvarez joins Lionel Messi among Argentina’s all-time top Champions League scorers

Julian Alvarez has joined Lionel Messi among the most prolific Argentine scorers in Champions League history. The Atletico Madrid striker scored in the 1-1 draw at Stuttgart, ending a long scoring drought and lifting his career total in the competition to 26 goals.

Atletico Madrid went into the break trailing after Leweling put Stuttgart ahead in the 36th minute. Alvarez answered early in the second half, finishing a cross from Lookman first time into the top corner in the 47th minute, scoring his first goal of the season for Atletico.

The goal also ended the longest drought of Alvarez’s career. He had not scored since Apr. 29, when he found the net in the 1-1 draw against Arsenal in last season’s Champions League semifinal, a gap of 174 days.

Coach Diego Simeone was full of praise for his forward after the match, especially for the effort he put in on the night. “The game he played was with incredible physical effort,” Simeone said. “He worked parts of the match as an inside forward on the left. The goal was a golazo and it makes me very happy for him, we need him.”

The Atletico Madrid manager also stressed how important the World Cup winner is to his team. Simeone described Alvarez as “decisive in getting to the places we need to reach” and made a request: “Let’s hope that little by little he starts to find himself again with what he showed us today”.

Lionel Messi is three games away from matching the longest goal contribution streak of his career

With the goal, Alvarez moved past Hernan Crespo, who sits on 25, and now ranks among the top five Argentine scorers in Champions League history. Only Messi, Alfredo Di Stefano, Sergio Aguero and Lautaro Martinez have scored more.

Alvarez will now try to build on his goal as Atletico Madrid look to turn their Champions League campaign around. With four points from three matches, the team will need its forward at his best, and the numbers suggest he is on his way to climbing even higher on Argentina’s all-time list.

The Como midfielder delivered a memorable performance against Manchester United, producing a night of individual brilliance that revived comparisons with his legendary compatriot and brought an impressive piece of Champions League history back into focus.

The England captain continued his impressive European campaign against Arsenal, delivering another decisive performance that has put the spotlight firmly on a record Ronaldo established during his Real Madrid career.

Manchester United manager Michael Carrick offered a direct apology to traveling supporters and shouldered collective blame after an abject 4-1 Champions League defeat to Italian newcomers Como.

Bukayo Saka was substituted off early against Bayern Munich after appearing to hurt his left knee while making a pass.

#3605. Jennifer Lawrence's Candid Confessions Remain Classic for a Reason

Jennifer Lawrence's candor was a huge part of her appeal as she rose to fame, and the Oscar-winning mother of two remains our favorite source of unfiltered commentary on any topic.

Along with the Oscar-caliber acting skills she displayed almost upon arrival in Hollywood 20 years ago, the now 36-year-old quickly became a reliable source of fabulously unfiltered commentary, her takes on pretty much everything—be it fame, appearance, bodily functions or Larry David—never failing to shock and delight.

But while becoming a walking pull quote came with great success, Lawrence pre-read the room and took a big step back from the spotlight in 2018.

"I needed a break from it," the Die My Love star said on a November 2025 episode of the New York Times' The Interview podcast, referring to her decade of headline ubiquity. "People needed a break from it. It was a mutual breakup.”

When she surfaced to promote Don't Look Up in 2021, she admitted to feeling some trepidation, quipping to Vanity Fair, "I’m nervous for you. I’m nervous for me. I’m nervous for the readers!"

Well, we're happy to report that Lawrence—while keeping certain boundaries in place to protect the private world she shares with husband Cooke Maroney and their kids Cy, 4, and his 19-month-old brother—re-emerged as her funny AF self when it comes to being a reliable font of remarks to chew on and savor.

For instance, she shared with The Cut in an interview published Oct. 27 that, while personally testing the products while developing her new hair care line, Mes, she left an unscented mask on for four days and started to smell like fish.

Lawrence recalled, "My husband was like, 'If you don't wash your hair, you have to get a hotel room.'"

And though Mes is purposefully pronounced "mess," the Hunger Games alum's foray into the beauty world is all in service of being a "sexy mom."

"At drop-off," she explained, "I want to look like a woman that has her s--t together even when I've just woken up and don't have my s --t together."

And if her hair looks good, Lawrence noted, "then nobody's gonna notice anything else about my face, clothes or body. I also can find myself using it to hide."

We appreciate her honesty, as always. Keep reading to see more of Lawrence's finest candid quotes:

When she was nominated for her first Best Actress Oscar at 20, for the 2010 indie drama Winter's Bone, Jennifer Lawrence made quite the impression right out of the gate, setting the tone for endless interviews to come.

"I just ate a Philly cheese steak and I was kinda worried about it," the star, clad in her instant-classic red Calvin Klein dress, shared with Entertainment Tonight on her first Oscars red carpet when told she looked incredible.

"You know, I think that people are built the way that they're built," she continued. "You know, there's that quote, the Kate Moss quote, that's like 'Nothing tastes as good as skinny feels'? "And I'm like, I can name a lot of things that taste better than skinny feels: potatoes, bread...a Philly cheese steak and fries!"

This is not the outfit Lawrence was wearing when, in her words, she stalked John Stamos at a party.

"He was at a party, and I turned into a perverted guy," she said on Conan in 2013. "I was like following him into rooms and staring at his ass."

Lawrence explained, "I lost my mind. And I got so upset—I didn't want to meet him because when I meet people it's never good, and I was avoiding him. But then my friends brought him over because my friends are jerks, and I started really freaking out. And he asked me if I was on mushrooms and I said, 'No. I'm dead sober. This is just me...'

"I just kept smiling and being like, 'Dude... Uncle Jesse.'"

Nick Jonas alerted Stamos to the interview by tweeting at him, "I envy you," to which Stamos replied, "I envy myself!" The Full House star added, "For the first time, I was totally oblivious."

"You guys are just standing up because you feel bad that I fell—and that's really embarrassing, but thank you," the Silver Linings Playbook star said upon finally reaching the stage to accept her Oscar for Best Performance by an Actress in a Leading Role in 2013, making the 22-year-old the second-youngest best actress winner ever.

Though the incident did indeed establish her reputation as a stumbler, the standing ovation was for a role well played.

"Was that on purpose? Absolutely," Lawrence joked backstage, Oscar in hand. "What do you mean, 'what happened?' Look at my dress," she added, pointing to the massive skirt of her Dior ballgown. "I tried to walk upstairs in this dress. That's what happened...Yeah, I think I just stepped on the fabric, and they wax the stairs."

Asked what went through her mind in that moment, she replied, "A bad word that I can't say, that starts with 'F.'"

Recalling the moment a year later to W, Lawrence explained, "I was at the Oscars, waiting to hear if my name was called, and I kept thinking, Cakewalk, cakewalk, cakewalk. I thought, Why is 'cakewalk' stuck in my head? And then, as I started to walk up the stairs and the fabric from my dress tucked under my feet, I realized my stylist had told me, 'Kick, walk, kick, walk.' You are supposed to kick the dress out while you walk, and I totally forgot because I was thinking about cake! And that's why I fell.'"

Lawrence later wondered if the fall was the beginning of the end.

She already felt that her perch at the top of the Hollywood food chain was "precarious," noting on the New York Times' The Interview podcast in 2025, "That’s just the nature of things."

"And then I fell getting my Oscar," she continued, "and the next year I was waving to fans and I tripped on a cone and I remember being like: “[Expletive], that’s it. Nobody’s gonna believe that I fell two years in a row.”

So, when she was accused of staging a fall, "Everybody thought that meant everything that I did was fake and it was all a shtick. This is how it felt to me. That I got found out as this fraud."

Whether she knew she was in the frame or not, the Hunger Games star zeroed in on Taylor Swift at the 2014 Golden Globes with a flourish.

"I was going to come in and push you down the stairs," Lawrence quipped when she crashed Swift's interview with E!'s Ryan Seacrest. "I was like, 'She'll crack up. She would really love that.'"

We haven't stopped thinking about Lawrence calling the crease that naturally forms under one's arm her "armpit vagina" at the 2014 SAG Awards.

Lawrence had a raging crush on Larry David, but you could almost hear the "...eh" coming from the Seinfeld and Curb Your Enthusiasm creator.

"I'm in love with him, and I have been for a very long time," she told Vanity Fair in 2014. "I worship Woody Allen, but I don't feel it below the belt the way I do for Larry David."

When those quotes were read back to him during a New Yorker Festival Q&A, David, who's 43 years her senior, replied, "I don't think I could do it. On one hand, it's very flattering and on another hand, it's kind of a shame—in terms of timing. I'd have fun watching the reality show of it, though."

Her love for the Real Housewives franchise is real, so of course she and her pals made a sojourn to the since-shuttered Pump in West Hollywood, part of Lisa Vanderpump's restaurant empire, where they created their own intro for spin-off Vanderpump Rules.

Someone was excited to see the iconic funnyman making his Comic-Con debut in 2015.

"I've been a big fan of Bill Murray for a really long time," Lawrence told reporters at a press conference following the cast's Mockingjay Part 2 panel in Hall H. "I sent emails to Woody, mostly when I was drunk, about things I always wanted to say to Bill Murray. So, he received them. The fact that Bill Murray knows that I exist was part one, part two was, he wanted to speak to me."

Lawrence made no secret of her obsession with the reigning first family of reality TV, even revealing that she watched endless repeats of Keeping Up With the Kardashians while shooting mother! so she could decompress from the intense role.

She told Vogue in 2017, "It got to a point in the movie where it got so dark I was like ya know what? I need the Kardashians."

Cue Lawrence FaceTiming into KUWTK in 2019.

Rules of the sky be damned, Lawrence commandeered the mic to lead her fellow airplane passengers in a cheer for the Philadelphia Eagles ahead of Super Bowl LII—which they won.

How She Really Felt About Her Wedding Day

"I'm so happy I didn't freak out and cancel the wedding and run away and go, 'I'll never be taken down!'" Lawrence cracked to the New York Times in 2021.

But, in all honesty, "It's so stressful," she told E!'s Keltie Knight at the 2024 Golden Globes about the day she wed gallerist Cooke Maroney in 2019. "You're not having fun. You're just like, 'Is that person having fun?'"

Asked what she'd been up to during her acting hiatus, the visibly pregnant Lawrence told Stephen Colbert in December 2021, "I just had a ton of sex."

“I was not pumping out the quality that I should have,” Lawrence, who put acting on the back burner in 2018 after several of her films in a row were met with underwhelming responses, told Vanity Fair in 2021. “I just think everybody had gotten sick of me. I’d gotten sick of me. It had just gotten to a point where I couldn’t do anything right. If I walked a red carpet, it was, ‘Why didn’t she run?’"

Why She Identifies as a "Stay-at-Home Mom"

“I identify as a stay-at-home mom," Lawrence, who shares two young sons with husband Cooke Maroney, said on a January 2026 episode of SmartLess. "But I am obviously working."

She explained, “I’m not used to being busy like this. I normally have a busy three months while I’m filming, but even in that sense, it’s calm because there is nothing else to do. I just go to work, come home, sleep, and then I do it again. And then, for two weeks, you promote. It is hectic.”

"There’s no squeezing [in work] when you have a baby," Lawrence told fellow mom Cameron Diaz in a chat for Interview in 2024. "There’s just home, and it’s the best. It definitely helps weed out projects: 'Yes. No. Yes. No. Yes. No. Is this worth being away from my child for half the day?'"

"Fortunately," she added, "my husband is the greatest father in the entire world, so when I’m working, I don’t have any more guilt than the usual every day, all-day parent guilt."

The company provided guidance for higher truck deliveries and steady margins in the fourth quarter. Management discussed investments in the PACCAR Connect platform and preparations for 2027 emissions regulations. Cash generation through the first nine months remained high at $2.52 billion, which exceeded reported net income for the period.

#3602. O.J. Simpson Doc Moved Up At Peacock Following NYT Investigation

OJ’s Accomplice, a two-part documentary that has been in the works at Peacock for some time, is having its premiere moved up as news around the infamous murder trial heats up.

The doc, which comes from Ari Mark’s Ample Entertainment, is set to premiere on the NBCUniversal streamer on November 1, having previously been dated for December.

The move comes after the New York Times published its own investigation around the case earlier this month with the streamer wanting to take advantage of the topic being in the zeitgeist. Related Stories News WNBA Moves Game 6 Of Final Series To Mid-Day Thursday Timeslot Due To Arena Shuffle News 'Southern Charm's Craig Conover To Star In Bravo Microdrama 'Charmed By A Southern Monster' Set For Peacock Premiere

It emerged last month that Peacock had ordered OJ’s Accomplice, a doc that claims to offer “real evidence” that suggests members of O.J. Simpson’s inner circle may have known more than they’ve let on about the brutal murders of Nicole Brown Simpson and Ron Goldman. Watch on Deadline

The NYT subsequently published a news feature based on records that show a “prized government informant had told law enforcement key information about the killings, including the supposed location of the murder weapon and details about Mr. Simpson’s efforts to evade prosecution”.

The streamer said its documentary “challenges the dominant narrative around the O.J. Simpson murder saga and offers real evidence suggesting that what we thought we knew about the most obsessed-over crime story of all time could be all wrong.”

It will feature interviews with Simpson’s houseguest and trial witness Kato Kaelin and Nicole Brown’s sister alongside never-before-heard first-person accounts from eyewitnesses and presents new information “raising questions as to whether members of O.J.’s inner circle may have been there that night.”

Others interviewed include the original defense investigator, Pat McKenna; lead detective Tom Lange; and DEA agent Robert Brisolari.

Nicole Brown Simpson – O.J.’s ex-wife – and Ron Goldman were murdered on June 12, 1994. Former NFL star and actor Simpson was charged with the murders and became a fugitive after failing to turn himself in to the L.A. Police Department and instead took off in a white Ford Bronco on a slow-speed chase that was watched live by millions.

However, Simpson was acquitted of both murders in 1995 after an eight-month trial, though Goldman’s father filed a civil suit against the Naked Gun star and a jury found him responsible for the deaths.

OJ’s Accomplice is directed by Mark and Sumit David, who also exec produce.

#3601. ‘OJ’s Accomplice’: Peacock Moves Up Documentary Release Date, Drops First Look (TV News Roundup)

O.J. Simpson documentary “OJ’s Accomplice” will premiere on Peacock Nov. 1, exploring the possibility that the former football star didn’t act alone the night he was accused of the 1994 murders.

Previously set to debut in December, the streaming platform moved up the release date of the two-part documentary that revisits the murders of Simpson’s wife Nicole Brown Simpson, and her friend, Ron Goldman. Peacock has also released a first look, featuring footage from inside the courtroom and a new eyewitness account. Related Stories 'Matchbox: The Movie' Review: John Cena Can't Make This Toy-Car Spinoff Feel Grownup 'Avatar: Seven Havens' Takes the Franchise in an Intriguing, Postapocalyptic New Direction: TV Review

“OJ’s Accomplice” will re-examine the crime scene and key evidence while also conducting interviews with the original defense investigator, Pat McKenna; lead detective Tom Lange; DEA agent Robert Brisolari; Simpson’s original defense team; A.C. Cowlings’ former roommate, Kato Kaelin; and Nicole Brown’s sister, Tanya Brown, to unfold what really happened on the night of June 12, 1994.

The documentary is directed by Ari Mark, Sumit David and produced by Ample Entertainment, with the duo also executive producing for Ample Entertainment. Sphere Abacus oversees all licensing and distribution.

“Ronin 3: The Battle for Sangin,” a three-part documentary series exploring the U.S. Marine’s 2010 fight in Sangin, Afghanistan, premiered Tuesday on Prime Video.

Narrated by Tom Berenger, the documentary is told through the Marines of the 3rd Battalion, 5th Marine Regiment who were stationed in Sangin District in Afghanistan’s Helmand Province. By the end of their deployment, 25 Marines from the battalion were killed in action and more than 200 were wounded. Never-before-seen battlefield footage and firsthand interviews from 25 Marines are used to depict one of the most costly U.S. Marine Corps deployments of the Afghanistan War.

The series is written, directed and produced by military journalist and filmmaker Jeanne McKinney. Patriot Profiles Productions produces, with McKinney and Dave McKinney serving as producers and executive producers alongside Aaron Semmel.

Muck Media has partnered with novelist Gary Shteyngart to develop his forthcoming essay collection “The Sensualist: Adventures in Pure Pleasure” into a documentary.

The series will see the writer travel the globe in pursuit of the idea that savoring life is an act of resistance. Shteyngart will follow the world’s largest rodent in South America, board the biggest cruise ship and travel to Naples, Havana, Bombay and Beijing.

“Adapting ‘The Sensualist’ feels less like a development decision and more like a moral obligation,” said director Darren Foster, partner at MUCK Media and series producer. “Once you see the world through Gary’s sensualist lens, you can’t unsee it — it’s the perfect tonic for our hangdog times. Comic, hyper-observant, self-lacerating, worldly and unexpectedly tender, this is Gary Shteyngart as a radical pied-piper: making you believe that pleasure, properly pursued, might actually save us all.”

“The Sensualist: Adventures in Pure Pleasure” will publish next month from Random House.

#3600. PHP2Shell: Exploitation attempts and remediation trends

Wiz research has been monitoring PHP2Shell exploitation attempts, but none have succeeded so far. We examine the techniques used, what prevented the attacks from completing and how observed exposure has changed since disclosure.

Last week, we published a report describing two major vulnerabilities in PHP (CVE-2026-109618, CVE-2026-109619). Since then, alongside much of the security industry, the Wiz Research team has been monitoring for related activity in the wild and reviewing publicly available exploit code. So far, we have observed a limited number of exploitation attempts in our telemetry and have not identified a publicly available proof of concept (PoC) demonstrating complete, general end-to-end exploitation against an arbitrary target. Some partners have, however, privately disclosed to us exploits that work against a variety of targets and we continue to experiment internally. Videos demonstrating our successful exploitations are available at php2shell.com.

This post summarizes what we have observed in terms of remediation efforts and exploitation attempts across real world environments and in our own honeypot data to date and shares findings from our ongoing investigation into these activities.

We will continue monitoring developments and update this post as new evidence emerges.

Following PHP2Shell’s public disclosure on October 21, 2026, Wiz telemetry has recorded an approximate 67% decrease in the daily number of distinct Internet-exposed machines vulnerable to PHP2Shell over the past few days. Separately, among vulnerable Internet-exposed machines, the proportion running PHP version 7.3 or earlier fell from 8.3% to 5.7% over a period of five days. This measures the share of the observed population running older versions that did not receive official backports.

During this period, the number of significantly affected organizations - defined here as those with at least one exposed and vulnerable instance - fell by approximately 47.9% (see graph below). For reference, our data shows that PHP itself is in use by 65% of organizations in our dataset.

Together, these observations are consistent with organizations collectively reducing their exposure through a combination of patching, upgrading, retiring affected machines and restricting internet access (note that this data shows the overall trend, but does not establish the relative contribution of each remediation strategy; scan coverage and size of observed population can also affect the overall reported numbers over time).

We have so far observed the following types of activities in this context:

Attempts using the PHP2Shell exploit mechanism, with techniques matching those of public PoCs.

PHP2Shell-branded tools unrelated to the vulnerabilities that instead relied on session poisoning and a separate local file inclusion (LFI) vulnerability in the application.

We observed two variants of an open source exploit kit targeting PHP-based software. Neither completed the exploit chain in the attempts we captured. The older variant encountered request-size limits or failed to produce the expected memory-exhaustion condition. The newer variant did consistently exhaust PHP’s memory, but failed to obtain the memory addresses needed to continue.

The first request to our PHP-CGI honeypot came from 3.37.227[.]134. It matched the kit’s older chain, found under archive/legacy-8m/exploit.py and its accompanying stage scripts. The first full exploit stage used a multipart body containing 1,005 parts, including a field named big filled with approximately 12 MB of padding:

According to the kit’s notes, this stage attempts to exhaust memory during a session reset, leaving the session state available for a later request.

On our honeypot, it failed before reaching that code. The declared request size exceeded the configured 8 MB post_max_size limit, so PHP skipped processing the multipart body and logged:

The author has since moved this chain into the kit’s archive directory.

In a later attempt, 103.151.172[.]95 sent a longer sequence from the legacy chain. It included stalled connections intended to occupy other Apache workers.

When only one worker was available the exploit attempted heap-shaping by sending requests containing 1,001 fields of 3,000 bytes each. These requests didn’t trigger the memory exhaustion flow required. So there is no evidence that this attempt reached the intended memory-exhaustion condition.

Most IP addresses we captured used the newer chain, built from pure/mk_a1.py and pure/mk_leak_b.py. We observed this variant in multiple attempts.

This variant progressed further: its first stage consistently caused PHP to exhaust its memory. The following stage, which attempts to leak process memory, failed in every run we captured.

The server logged a fatal error indicating that PHP reached its limit, for example:

The chain depends on consecutive exploit requests reaching the same Apache child process. To increase the chances of that happening, the kit attempts to occupy the other workers with incomplete requests. Each connection declares a large body, sends a single byte and stalls:

One address, 178.249.214[.]6, opened 399 of these connections alongside 400 GET requests before sending the exploit stages. The address is a VPN exit node, which limits what we can infer about the source.

A1 is a chunked multipart request containing a null-byte session ID, 860 empty file parts with long, deeply nested array names and several groups of padding fields:

The request is designed to exhaust memory during session handling at a point that can leave the session ID pointing to freed memory.

Every A1 request we captured returned HTTP 500, accompanied by a PHP error matching:

This confirms that A1 reached the intended memory-exhaustion condition on our honeypot. It does not, by itself, confirm that the stale session state needed by the next stage was preserved.

B1 followed roughly a second later. It sent another chunked multipart request containing PHPSESSID=a, PHP_SESSION_UPLOAD_PROGRESS=k0and two large padding fields.

The failed leaks highlight a broader challenge: building a generalized exploit that works reliably across PHP versions and deployment configurations is difficult. The kit’s B1 layout was calculated for the official php:8.4.26-apache image with a 128 MB memory limit. Other instances like our Roundcube honeypot ran the same PHP version from a different build, with additional extensions and a 64 MB memory limit. These differences can affect heap layout and memory reuse, potentially preventing the leak from working as expected.

Reliable worker pinning adds another challenge. Consecutive exploit requests must reach the same Apache child process while the required session state persists. Request timeouts, network latency and worker availability can disrupt this sequence. In our own testing, request timeouts combined with network latency were enough to prevent the chain from completing over an internet connection.

So far, we have not identified a publicly available, generalized solution for either worker pinning or information leaking across different deployments. Simply copying public PoCs and running them at scale does not address the work needed to turn a configuration-specific exploit into a reliable operational kit. We expect these barriers to diminish over time: some threat actors have the expertise needed to adapt the chain and build more robust tooling.

We also saw failed exploitation attempts from 15.204.59[.]70 and 162.35.99[.]94, but these scanners did successfully exploit Adel Karimi's honeypot, which was a likely contributing factor to CISA’s decision to include PHP2Shell in their KEV list.

We also observed tools labeled as PHP2Shell exploits that did not actually attempt to exploit either vulnerability. The earliest such traffic, from 85.11.187[.]35, matched a Go port of “Chain A” from WoYinDao/CVE-2026-109618-CVSS-9.2-Critical.

The tool used a longstanding session-poisoning technique, attempting to write PHP code into a session file through the upload-progress feature. The request below is abbreviated:

It then attempted to execute that code by including the session file:

Crucially, lfi_sink.php is a vulnerable endpoint supplied by the PoC’s lab environment. The tool assumes that this endpoint exists on the target; without it or an equivalent local file inclusion (LFI) vulnerability, the chain cannot execute code.

A padded variant triggered our memory-exhaustion detection, but we observed no successful code execution. Despite the PHP2Shell branding, this activity relied on an unrelated technique and a vulnerable application endpoint.

We have not yet observed successful end-to-end PHP2Shell exploitation in our own telemetry. However, Adel Karimi’s public report and supporting data provide the most robust public evidence we have seen to date, correlating exploit traffic, memory disclosure and subsequent code execution on his honeypots. Notably, those targets used a favorable single-worker Apache mod_php configuration, so the findings do not establish reliability across other deployments.

Our observations show that triggering memory exhaustion alone is not enough to complete the exploit chain. Differences in PHP builds, memory limits and worker behavior can prevent subsequent stages from working. The challenge for opportunistic attackers is turning currently available public PoCs into similarly reliable operational tooling or developing it on their own. In our view, PHP2Shell remains a risk in both the short and long term and applying the PHP security updates or remediating through other means therefore remains essential.

GitHub's 2026 Actions changes make pull_request_target attacks harder, but not impossible. Here's the residual attack surface and how to audit for it.

An automated campaign is planting secret-dumping workflows in every repository that stolen GitHub tokens can reach. It uses no C2 server, is actively evolving, and has been significantly disrupted by recent GitHub security improvements.

Detect and mitigate CVE-2026-111497, a critical Brotli decoder vulnerability enables remote code execution.

#3599. 'Big Brother' Star Amber Siyavus Found Dead With Drugs, Paraphernalia Nearby, Police Say

"Big Brother" star Amber Siyavus was found dead with a mysterious white powder and drug paraphernalia near her body ... TMZ has learned.

According to a Las Vegas Metropolitan Police Department report obtained by TMZ, officers responded to a home on October 9 after Amber was discovered unresponsive in a bedroom.

Police say Amber was found holding what appeared to be a white snorting straw and a lighter. When her body was moved, officers discovered aluminum foil with burnt residue and a small plastic bag containing white powder underneath her.

Officers also found numerous prescription pills in Amber's luggage, along with four Narcan sprays, which are used to reverse opioid overdoses.

Amber's aunt told police the former reality star had returned to Las Vegas from Michigan on October 4 to visit her mom. Her aunt said she wasn't aware of Amber having any suicidal thoughts, adding that Amber had been in Michigan trying to move her son to Las Vegas.

The report also reveals Amber had a history of drug use, with her aunt telling investigators Amber previously attended rehab for heroin and unidentified pills.

Her aunt said she first spotted Amber lying sideways in bed around 7 AM but didn't think much of it and went back to sleep. When she checked again around 11 AM, Amber was still in the same position and wasn't responding, prompting her to call police.

Dispatchers were told Amber wasn't breathing and was cold to the touch. She was pronounced dead at 1:40 PM.

Amber's official cause and manner of death have not been revealed.

Amber competed on Season 8 of "Big Brother" in 2007, where her emotional moments earned her the nickname "Whamber." She finished in seventh place.

#3598. If You Loved 'Crystal Lake,' There's One Movie You Need to Watch Next

Jeff Somers is a freelancer who has been writing about writing, books, personal finance, and home maintenance since 2012.

Jeff has published nine novels and more than 70 short stories; his story Ringing the Changes was selected for inclusion in The Best American Mystery Stories 2006. In 2018, Jeff published the book Writing Without Rules: How to Write & Sell a Novel Without Guidelines, Experts, or (Occasionally) Pants. He holds a bachelor’s degree in English and previously worked in medical publishing as a production editor.

When not writing, Jeff spends his free time fixing up his old house. He has tackled a wide range of DIY projects, including remodeling a bathroom entirely on his own in two weeks, and building a roof deck that hasn't fallen down yet. You can find him on Bluesky.

This is Streamalikes, where you can find a curated list of recommendations to stream based on what you already like.

Peacock’s Crystal Lake is an unexpected treat in a lot of ways, taking one of horror’s most iconic figures—Jason Voorhees of the Friday the 13th franchise—and digging into the universe’s background by focusing on his mother, Pamela. Famously the actual serial killer in the original film, Pamela’s development into a psychotic killer who inspired her son to become the psychotic killer of all time is the engine that drives Crystal Lake, finding the damaged humanity behind the terror. If you’ve burned through all eight episodes of Crystal Lake’s first season, and you’ve worked through the streamalike series with similar DNA, it’s time to check out some movies—starting with the perfect companion film, 2022’s Pearl.

The second film in Ti West’s X trilogy (starting with X and concluding with 2024’s MaXXXine) is the ideal film to watch after Crystal Lake. Like the series, it explores a female serial killer’s backstory and recontextualizes your understanding of their motives and psychology. It also requires that you be familiar with the original story—while you can certainly watch Crystal Lake or Pearl without having seen the Friday the 13th films or X, you’ll be missing out on a lot if you do.

In X, a group of young people descends on a dilapidated old farm to film an adult movie in Texas in 1979, and by the end of the film most of them are dead, killed by the farm’s elderly couple Howard (Stephen Ure) and Pearl (Mia Goth), with Pearl presented as the most deranged and dangerous of the two. It’s a riveting update to the classic splatter movie template, and Goth nails her dual role as the Final Girl Maxine and the psychotic, murderous Pearl. The prequel, Pearl, jumps back to 1918, when Pearl (Goth again) was a young woman living with her strict mother and paralyzed father. Pearl has big dreams of being an entertainer, but her mother tries to trap her to ensure she’s there to care for her parents and work the farm. Pearl’s lonely unhappiness and frustration (both mental and, er, physical) inspire a slow boil of tension as her grip on sanity frays, and her anger toward her parents curdles into something frightening.

Like Crystal Lake, Pearl takes a killer who seems fairly one-dimensional in their original presentation and gives them a deeper back story and psychological framing. Pamela Voorhees wasn’t just crazy—she was driven to madness by the horrible things that happened to her and her son, and the awful way she’s treated by her fellow humans. Similarly, Pearl is a hopeful and innocent young woman who is slowly molded into a killer by the events of her life. Both the series and the film serve as reminders that monsters are rarely born—they’re made. And it’s usually the casual cruelty of the people around them that does the making. If you loved Crystal Lake’s fresh take on Pamela, Pearl is the perfect choice for a movie night. Stream Pearl on Hulu or rent it on Prime Video.

Need more serial killer movies that hit the Crystal Lake vibe? Never fear—here are some other excellent choices to pair with the series.

If you’ve never actually seen the original Friday the 13th (or if it’s been a while), this is a must-watch for fans of Crystal Lake. Although the series takes some liberties to update the timeline, it’s still intended as a glimpse into the events that shaped both Pamela and Jason Voorhees into inhuman (and somewhat supernatural) monsters. Initially given savagely bad reviews, the film was a surprise blockbuster and is now regarded as a classic of a genre it helped to create in the first place; rewatching it today not only helps contextualize Crystal Lake, it puts the entire slasher genre into perspective. Stream Friday the 13th on Netflix or rent it on Prime Video.

The success of Friday the 13th led to a rush of copycat films, of course. Most of those weren’t particularly good or even interesting, but Sleepaway Camp is an exception (although it was similarly savaged by critics at the time). Now a cult classic, Sleepaway Camp has two things that recommend it to fans of Crystal Lake and Friday the 13th: a weird sensibility that gives it an otherworldly atmosphere that enhances the sense of dread, and what is probably the most unexpected twist ending in the history of slasher films (and it’s probably a Top 10 twist in general). Stream Sleepaway Camp on The Roku Channel, Kanopy, or Tubi, or rent it on Prime Video.

One reason Crystal Lake exists is the enduring power and influence of slasher films in general and Friday the 13th specifically. Few recent films have tapped into that dreamy nightmare feel like Jane Schoenbrun’s Teenage Sex and Death at Camp Miasma. As with most of Schoenbrun’s work, the film delves into themes of identity, subverting queer tropes, and the malleable nature of reality itself. If you’re looking for a slasher story that’s less straightforward than Crystal Lake while dealing with some similar themes of transformation and destiny, this is it. Stream Teenage Sex and Death at Camp Miasma on Mubi.

If you’ve seen all the Friday the 13th movies and want more slasher horror with a bit more edge, check out The Burning. Is it a great film? Not really. Combining the straightforward slasher thrills of Friday with some of the villain motivations of fellow classic franchise A Nightmare on Elm Street, The Burning dials the blood and guts up to 11 (and beyond) and remains notoriously gory even by today’s standards. But if your fave part of Crystal Lake is the frequently over-the-top gore and violence, this is an ideal movie choice. Stream The Burning on The Roku Channel or Kanopy.

Want a slasher film that explores the psyche of a female killer the way Crystal Lake deconstructs Pamela Voorhees? Check out May. A fragile young woman, May struggles to find connection with the people around her, and slowly descends into a fantasy world that inspires her to literally make her own ideal friend. What makes May so interesting is the depth of the exploration of May’s personality that’s similar to how Pamela is handled in Crystal Lake, tracing each step of her stumble into a bloody hell. Stream May on Tubi or rent it on Prime Video.

#3597. Neil Young Really Wants Trump to Stop Playing “Rockin’ in the Free World”

Stop me if you’ve heard this one before: Neil Young is filing suit after “Rockin’ in the Free World” was played at a Trump rally. The lawsuit—filed yesterday (Monday, October 26) in Virginia’s Eastern District Court—specifically cites an “unauthorized public performance” of Young’s 1991 protest anthem at a rally in Youngstown, Ohio on October 12. The named defendant is actually not President Donald J. Trump himself, but rather the Trump 47 Committee, his primary political action committee and the event’s organizer.

“‘Rockin’ in the Free World’ is a blistering anthem that contrasts the hollow promises of American political rhetoric with the harsh realities faced by the poor, homeless, and forgotten,” the lawsuit reads. “The lyrics paint vivid, street-level portraits—a mother discarding her baby, addicts lost in despair, garbage piling in the shadows of prosperity—while the repeated chorus delivers a savage irony, celebrating freedom that clearly isn’t shared by all. In short, the Song is the opposite of everything that Donald J. Trump stands for.” Pitchfork has reached out to Young’s legal representation for further comment.

Young is seeking $10 million in damages on the grounds of copyright infringement, asserting that the Trump 47 Committee did not have “license or Young’s permission” to play the song at any “purely political event.” He also demands that neither “Rockin’ in the Free World” nor any of his other songs be played at future events organized by or associated with the PAC.

During the 2020 presidential election, Young sued Trump’s campaign committee, Donald J. Trump for President Inc., over their use of “Rockin’ in the Free World” and another of his songs, “Devil’s Sidewalk,” at campaign rallies. The parties settled out of court under the stipulation, according to the new suit, that the corporation “would never use ‘Rockin’ in the Free World’ without a license and permission from Young.” However, as a separate organization, the Trump 47 Committee would be exempt from the terms of that specific settlement. Agh, technicalities!

#3596. Cristiano Ronaldo’s penalty miss blunder prompts touching reaction from Al-Nassr boss Ange Postecoglou amid Jorge Jesus fallout

Cristiano Ronaldo endured a frustrating night for Al-Nassr, but head coach Ange Postecoglou made a telling gesture that offered a glimpse into their relationship during a difficult period for the Portuguese superstar. With Portugal coach Jorge Jesus still at the center of a controversy surrounding Ronaldo’s international future, the Saudi club’s captain faced another testing evening as he tried to put recent turmoil behind him.

Al-Nassr ultimately secured a valuable victory over NEOM SC in the Saudi Pro League, thanks to a decisive contribution from Ronaldo’s Portuguese teammate Joao Felix. However, the result was accompanied by several missed opportunities from the five-time Ballon d’Or winner, leaving Postecoglou with questions about his finishing rather than the team’s overall performance.

Ronaldo had the opportunity to put Al-Nassr ahead shortly before half-time on Wednesday after a rival defender brought down Felix inside the penalty area. The 41-year-old stepped up to take the spot-kick but sent his effort wide, wasting a promising opportunity to break the deadlock.

His frustration continued after the interval, when another excellent chance presented itself inside the box. Ronaldo failed to connect properly with a close-range opportunity, while another wayward effort later in the match compounded an unusually wasteful performance.

The missed penalty was Ronaldo’s fourth from 31 attempts for Al-Nassr since joining the Saudi club in 2023. Despite his disappointing finishing, the forward played the full 90 minutes as his team continued its push near the top of the league table.

While the 41-year-old superstar struggled in front of goal, Felix provided the moment that ultimately decided the contest. The Portugal international struck from the edge of the penalty area in the 61st minute, securing a 1-0 victory over NEOM and extending Al-Nassr’s winning run. The result marked the club’s seventh league victory of the season, lifting Postecoglou’s side to third place, three points behind leaders Al Qadsiah with a game in hand.

The most memorable moment came after the final whistle, when the Australian coach approached Ronaldo and put his arm around the disappointed captain, offering words of encouragement rather than publicly criticizing his missed chances. A video of the interaction circulated on social media, with supporters praising the manager for standing by his star player during a difficult evening.

Postecoglou reinforced that message during his press conference, explaining that mistakes were part of the sport and that the squad needed to respond collectively. His comments reflected a supportive approach at a time when Ronaldo was already facing scrutiny over his performances and his international situation.

“This is the nature of football, and it is good that we create this number of chances, and we will work on making up for the mistakes,” the Australian explained. He continued: “We work as a family, so a win will be good for everyone and a loss will fall upon the whole team, so we must support one another and move forward with steady steps.”

Ronaldo’s difficult evening came shortly after the Portuguese Football Federation (FPF) imposed a 30-day suspension and a €255 ($286) fine, following his departure from the national team camp without permission. The incident reportedly followed tensions involving Portugal coach Jorge Jesus, leaving Ronaldo’s immediate international future under discussion.

Head coach Jorge Jesus interacts with Cristiano Ronaldo of Al Nassr. (Yasser Bakhsh/Getty Images)

The suspension could be backdated to his provisional ruling, potentially allowing him to return for Portugal’s next match against Denmark on November 14. Meanwhile, Postecoglou has continued to express his admiration for Ronaldo, describing the opportunity to work with one of soccer’s greatest players as a major attraction of the Al-Nassr job.

Al Nassr face Uzbekistan's Neftchi Fergana in Matchday 2 of the AFC Champions League Elite, and head coach Ange Postecoglou has provided a key update on Cristiano Ronaldo's availability.

Cristiano Ronaldo could reportedly reunite with Roberto Martínez at Al Nassr, according to a Saudi TV program that claims Ange Postecoglou’s future is in danger.

Ange Postecoglou says Cristiano Ronaldo is "fresh, fit and ready to play" for Al Nassr's match against Al Diriyah on Friday-

Postecoglou started life in Riyadh with five consecutive victories and quickly earned praise, but a series of disappointing results has now created questions about what happens next.

#3595. Freeport-McMoRan (FCX) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Freeport-McMoRan Inc. (FCX +1.21%) reported a significant increase in net income and operating cash flow for the third quarter, supported by a 40% rise in realized copper prices and the continued ramp-up of the Grasberg minerals district. Management confirmed a 20% increase in copper sales guidance for 2027 and provided updated capital expenditure estimates to support brownfield expansions and technology initiatives. The company reported steady progress on its Grasberg Block Cave operations and the restart of smelting facilities in Indonesia, which are expected to drive higher gold and copper sales in the fourth quarter. CFO Robertson noted that the company's financial policy remains focused on a strong balance sheet and balanced distributions, with over $6.6 billion returned to shareholders since 2021.

Operator: Ladies and gentlemen, thank you for standing by and welcome to the Freeport-McMoRan third quarter conference call. At this time, all participants are in a listen-only mode. Later, we will conduct a question and answer session. If you wish to ask a question during the Q&A session, press star one on your touch-tone phone. If you require assistance during the conference, please press star zero. I would now like to turn the conference over to Mr. David Joint, Vice President, Investor Relations. Please go ahead, sir.

Anyone may listen to the conference call by accessing our website homepage and clicking on the webcast link. A replay of the webcast will be available on our website later today. Richard will make some opening remarks, Kathleen and Maree will review our slide materials, and then we'll open up the call for questions. Richard?

Richard Adkerson: Thanks, David. We appreciate you all for joining us today. We've been looking forward to speaking with you about our strong third quarter results and our positive outlook for the future. We are pleased and proud of our team with the steady progress throughout the year achieved on the Grasberg ramp up and with our initiatives across our business to drive value. As we look forward, the table is set for growing our volumes in the near term, medium term, and longer term, supported by our portfolio of long-lived assets and by a qualified and experienced team. Leaders in Indonesia have expressed support for the extension of our operating rights beyond 2041.

We continue to work with the Ministry of Energy and Mines on documentation to implement the memorandum of understanding we signed with the government of Indonesia. Freeport's growth plans match up exceptionally well with accelerating requirements for copper. Electrification continues to advance at a rapid pace, bigger and faster than we've seen in the past, and copper is the metal of electrification. The world requires a lot more copper, and supplies are challenged. Freeport is particularly well-positioned to grow with the market. After working in this industry for decades, today's opportunity, where structural trends point to multiyear period of significant demand growth, is unique. We at Freeport have the right assets at the right time.

With a strong team in the Freeport family and our strong financial positions, we are well-positioned to capture this opportunity for the benefit of our shareholders and all our stakeholders. Kathleen?

Kathleen Quirk: Great. Thank you, Richard, and thank all of you for participating on today's call, where we'll review our third quarter performance and update you on our initiatives, projects, and future outlook. Today, we will be discussing our team's work across our global business to build value. Our solid progress and momentum to safely and sustainably restore large-scale production at Grasberg, drive operational excellence and new technology initiatives in the Americas, and advance plans for value-enhancing organic growth. Starting on slide three, we show the highlights of our third quarter performance. Copper sales have increased in each of the last three quarters, and we expect fourth quarter sales to be the strongest of the year by far.

Steady progress continues at Grasberg, with 67% ramp up achieved, and confidence is building toward full scale. Strong operational execution, combined with the favorable metal price backdrop, provided significant margins, cash flows, and earnings. The diversity of our portfolio and pricing leverage for our U.S. business comes through in the results. Operating income for our U.S. mining operations more than doubled in the third quarter compared with last year's third quarter, with strong conversion to the bottom line. This contributed to an increase of over 80% in our consolidated net income compared with the year ago quarter. The progress achieved during 2026 sets us up for growing volumes and improving financial performance as we look ahead to 2027 and 2028.

Additionally, we are advancing our future growth plans with a series of projects in progress. We returned nearly $1 billion to shareholders year-to-date, including $300 million in share purchases. Our financial position remains strong, and as we look ahead, growing volumes combined with a positive market environment for our products sets us up for significant increase in cash flows to support our value-enhancing investment program and increasing returns to shareholders. Moving to the copper markets on slide four. As a leading global supplier of refined copper, Freeport has strategic significance and is strongly positioned to benefit from copper's essential and increasingly important role in societies around the world.

Copper's superior conductivity makes it the metal of electrification, and the global economy is rapidly becoming more electrified. Globally, copper demand is expected to rise in a number of applications, including the massive requirements for the power grid to support new technologies. LME copper prices averaged $6.09 per pound year-to-date through September, and currently approximate $6.58 per pound on the LME, an increase of 16% since the start of the year. Our U.S. customers report robust copper demand associated with growing requirements for electricity and energy infrastructure, data center builds, and in the transportation sector.

Copper continues to be strategically significant to China as the world's largest consumer to support its domestic power grid, technology advancement, and manufacturing of autos and consumer goods for growing exports. The sizable movement of product to U.S. warehouses continues, resulting in tight market conditions in the major consuming regions outside of the U.S. The situation underscores the strategic role of copper in the global economy, and supply chains to support future growth. The signals we see are clearly pointing to requirements for additional copper supplies to meet growing demand.

At Freeport, we have a valuable, geographically diverse portfolio of integrated copper assets with large-scale production facilities that are difficult to replicate, long life reserves and resources, and a portfolio of low risk, brownfield expansion opportunities to serve a growing market. I'm going to move to the operating highlights on slide five, where we summarize the highlights by geographic region. Starting with the U.S., we continue to focus on operating disciplines to improve efficiencies and productivity to grow production despite low ore grades. We were successful in improving our mining rates compared with the year ago quarter.

However, an abnormally high frequency of extreme weather events in the third quarter, which have continued in the fourth quarter, disrupted some of the momentum achieved earlier in the year. We are deploying new technology tools to enhance our operating models and are investing in more efficient trucks and mining equipment to support our objective of increasing U.S. production and reducing costs. We continue to pursue an exceptional opportunity to unlock meaningful value through application of new technologies for recovering copper in our leaching processes.

Data from our work to date is supportive of a potential breakthrough, and we are looking forward to what we'll learn as we go through to 2027 as high-potential, next generation proprietary additives are deployed in the field. In South America, our flagship Cerro Verde operation in Peru continues to demonstrate solid operating performance and productivity consistent with a large-scale, world-class operation. At El Abra in Chile, ore grades and operating rates were impacted during the quarter by mine sequencing to address slope management. We're now commissioning new facilities at El Abra that will allow us to enhance metal production by increasing temperatures in our leach pads.

We continue to work with the Chilean government to advance our environmental impact study to position El Abra for a major transformative expansion. In Indonesia, we're pleased to report that the team continues to achieve meaningful progress on the ramp-up at the Grasberg Block Cave. We operated at 67% of mine capacity during the third quarter, and progress is advancing for meaningful growth as we go into 2027. Upgrades to the material handling system for our automated rail system are progressing on schedule, and we continue to advance work for a restart of production Block One South in 2027. We continue to target overall rates reaching 80% by mid-2027 and approach full capacity by the end of next year.

We were successful in restarting the smelter and now have both smelters in Indonesia in operation to support our mine ramp-up. The team is executing the phased ramp-up safely and efficiently, and we continue to de-risk the plans and increase confidence in a long-term outlook. In Indonesia, we continue to experience abnormally dry weather conditions and are closely monitoring forecasts which predict conditions to return to more normal levels as we move into 2027. The dry conditions are generally favorable for mining, but low water availability presents challenges in increasing mill rates. We are actively managing the situation with limited impacts to date.

As Richard mentioned, we are continuing to work cooperatively with the Indonesian government to secure our long-term operating extension in accordance with the MoU reached early in the year. We submitted our application in June and we are continuing to work to complete the formal process. The government continues to express support for extension to provide long-term continuity of large-scale operations and mutual benefits. Big picture, the third quarter, the team executed well, but experienced challenging operating conditions. You will note we continue to expect the fourth quarter will be the strongest of the year, but have adjusted our 2026 copper sales by approximately 2% to incorporate the impact of shortfalls in the U.S. and in Chile.

Our team is committed to execution, dedicated to overcoming challenges, and driven for excellence in all that we do. I am going to move to slide six, where we have some real excitement around Freeport about the growth catalyst. We are driven to increase copper supply to a growing market and are strongly positioned as we look forward with significant near-term, medium-term, and long-term growth. The progress we have made throughout 2026 positions us for significant growth in volumes, margins, and cash flows in 2027 and 2028, as Maree will cover shortly. In the short-term, we are positioned to grow from GBP 3 billion this year to GBP 3.7 billion in 2027 and over GBP 4 billion in 2028.

Continued strong execution of the Grasberg recovery is the major driver, with improved U.S. production adding incrementally. This growth notably does not require new capital. With our U.S. growth initiative as America’s Copper Champion, we see a path for Freeport to be a GBP 4.5 billion-GBP 5 billion a year copper producer in the 2030 timeframe, with success in our innovative leach initiative and our expansion of our Bagdad mine in Arizona. We continue to see the innovative leach initiative as one of the most attractive opportunities in our company, because it leverages our existing infrastructure, is scalable, is not capital intensive, and has low incremental operating costs.

We have been on this journey and advancing this initiative for the last three to four years, and our excitement continues to build, supported by progress in developing a proprietary next generation additive that would unlock recoveries of low-grade ores previously thought to be unrecoverable. As you will see in the chart, lab testing of the new Freeport additive is showing incremental recoveries of more than four times those achieved with our initial additive. The next generation additive is now being manufactured on a fast-track schedule and will start receiving commercial quantities for field deployment as we go into 2027.

While we need to be cautious until it's proven in the field, this could represent the major breakthrough technology that we've been working to achieve. We expect to make an investment decision on the Bagdad expansion in the fourth quarter. Very excited about this. We see it as an attractive opportunity to transform Bagdad into the second-largest copper mine in the U.S., benefiting from economies of scale and the use of modern technology to set the operation up for improved multi-decade performance.

We're also advancing further growth options, which could be developed by the mid-2030s with a large-scale expansion of the El Abra mine in Chile and exciting possibilities for development of our large resource position in the Safford Lone Star district in Arizona. We see our pipeline of organic growth being particularly attractive in the context of an industry where greenfield projects carry significant risk and development time frames can span 15-20 years. We have a differentiated, advantageous position since our projects are brownfield in nature, resulting in benefits from leveraging existing infrastructure, lower capital intensity, experienced workforces, and relationships with key stakeholders to move more quickly with less risk than a greenfield project.

We're increasingly mindful of escalating inflationary pressures affecting capital costs for major projects and the need to be disciplined on qualifying projects, delivering investment returns, and carefully controlling budgets and schedules. We're going to continue to be focused, selective, and deliberate as we go forward and on balancing investments and growth with shareholder returns. At Freeport, we benefit from an experienced and highly respected project development team with proven experience in successfully managing major projects around the world. Putting this all together, the setup for Freeport is exceptionally strong and Team Freeport is intensely focused on execution and delivery to capture the value and the special opportunities that lie ahead.

Now I'm going to turn the call to Maree, who will review our outlook, and then we'll take your questions.

Maree Robertson: Thanks, Kathleen. Just flipping to slide seven, we show our three-year outlook for sales volumes of copper, gold, and molybdenum. As Kathleen referenced, we expect significant production and sales growth in the coming years. We have trimmed our 2026 copper sales by about 2%, taking into account slightly lower production in the U.S. and Chile, and by approximately 2.5% in 2027 to reflect current mine plan updates. Copper sales in 2028 are consistent with previous estimates, and gold and moly sales are unchanged for the three-year period. We expect our fourth quarter of 2026 sales volumes to be the highest of the year, with copper sales increasing 17% and gold sales nearly tripling from third quarter levels.

For the year 2027, we expect annual growth in copper sales of over 20% compared with 2026 and more than a 50% increase in gold volumes, with additional growth in 2028 of 10% for copper and 20% for gold. Our team remains focused on delivering our plans safely and efficiently, which will translate into strong financial performance and meaningful future cash flow growth. Moving to slide eight, we continue to carefully manage all costs and capital whilst driving efficiency and productivity programs, particularly in light of inflationary pressures affecting our business and the industry.

As discussed on prior calls, we continue to experience cost pressures and volatility in global energy and commodity markets, including oil, sulfur, and acid, largely associated with the conflict in the Middle East. Rising energy costs during 2026, particularly diesel prices, have impacted costs by approximately $500 million compared with 2025. Sulfuric acid, a key input into various copper processing technologies across the industry, has more than doubled this year. Notably, Freeport is comparatively well-positioned given our integrated business model and is generally insulated from price moves as a significant producer of sulfuric acid through our smelting operations and consumer of acid in our leaching processes.

In addition to operating costs, capital costs across the mining industry have been trending at a greater pace than overall headline inflation rates in recent years. These pressures affect both sustaining capital and expansion project costs, reinforcing the importance of remaining highly focused on carefully assessing our priorities, pursuing continuous optimization programs, and maintaining rigorous and strict project and cost controls. On net unit cash costs, we are maintaining our full year guidance for 2026 of $1.90 per pound, with by-products, particularly molybdenum, providing an offset to the impact of higher input costs.

In putting together our projected volumes and cost estimates, we show modeled results on slide nine for EBITDA and cash flow at various copper prices, ranging from $5-$7 copper. These are modeled results using the average of 2027 and 2028 with current volume and cost estimates and holding gold flat at $4,000 per ounce and molybdenum flat at $32 per pound. Annual EBITDA would range from over $12 billion per annum at $5 copper to $19.5 billion at $7 copper, with operating cash flows ranging from over $9 billion per year at $5 to approximately $15 billion at $7 copper. We show sensitivities to various commodities on the right.

You'll note we are highly leveraged to copper prices, with each $0.10 per pound change equating to approximately $390 million in annual EBITDA in the 2027, 2028 periods. We also have exposure to gold prices with each $100 per ounce change in price approximating $105 million in annual EBITDA, and to molybdenum, which has shown significant price strength in recent months. Each dollar per pound change in moly approximates $85 million in EBITDA per annum. With our long life reserves and large scale production, we are well-positioned to generate substantial cash flows to fund future organic growth and cash returns under our performance-based payout framework. Slide 10 shows our current forecast for capital expenditure in 2026 and 2027.

Our 2026 capital expenditure is currently estimated to approximate $4.4 billion, a 2% increase from the prior estimate, and $5.2 billion for 2027, an increase of approximately $400 million above the July estimate, primarily reflecting additional mining equipment investments for supporting enhanced productivity improvements in the U.S. and cost inflation. The discretionary projects are expected to approximate $1.6 billion in 2026 and $2 billion in 2027, with roughly 50% related to the Kucing Liar development and the LNG project at Grasberg. The balance includes acceleration of tailings and other infrastructure to support the Bagdad expansion, upgraded mining equipment, and capitalized interest.

The discretionary category reflects the capital investments we are making in new projects that, under our financial policy, are funded with the 50% of available cash that is not distributed. These projects are value-enhancing initiatives and are detailed on slide 28 in our reference materials. The capital estimates exclude projects that remain subject to completion of final studies and board approval, including the potential Bagdad 2X expansion project. As I mentioned on the previous slide, we will continue to carefully manage our spending and deploy capital strategically to projects with the best return and risk-reward profiles.

Finally, on slide 11, we reiterate that financial policy priorities centered on a strong balance sheet, cash returns to shareholders, and investments in value-enhancing growth projects. Our balance sheet is solid with investment-grade ratings, robust credit metrics, and flexibility within our debt targets to execute on our projects. We have a strong liquidity position and have substantial flexibility for funding upcoming 2027 maturities. Our strong balance sheet and significant cash flow generation supports disciplined investment in future growth whilst returning cash to shareholders.

Since adopting our financial policy in 2021, we have distributed $6.6 billion to shareholders through dividends and share purchases, including approximately $1 billion year-to-date in 2026, and have an attractive future long-term portfolio that will enable us to continue to build long-term value for shareholders. Our global team is focused on disciplined execution, profitable growth, and long-term value creation. Thanks for your attention, and we will now turn to questions.

Operator: Ladies and gentlemen, we will now begin the question and answer session. If you wish to ask a question, press star one on your touch-tone phone. If your question has been answered or you wish to remove yourself from the queue, please press star one again. If you are using a speakerphone, please pick up your handset before pressing the numbers. We ask that you please limit your questions to one. If you have additional questions, please return to the queue. One moment, please, for our first question. Our first question will come from the line of Richard Garchitorena with Barclays. Please go ahead.

Richard Garchitorena: Great. Thanks, and congrats on the strong quarter. First, my question on Grasberg. Given the progress that you are seeing with ramp-up on track production from Block 1S expected for mid-2027 and then full run rates by the end of the year, how should we think about the cadence of production for copper and gold in the first half of next year versus second half? How should we think about the mine plan heading into 2028 as you ramp up even further?

But in places like Chino, where the leach recovery is higher to start with, you add 40% or more and you get closer to 80% or 85%. It depends on the ore types that are involved. The thing that could help us in addition to the residual copper is these additives are going to be helping us also with new placements. We're just going to have to wait, Brian.

Kathleen Quirk: we could be over or under 800. We've been through the model several times, and we feel that 800, it's aspirational, but you can model it based on what we know. Give us a few more quarters to give you more feedback specifically on what that could look like. The 200 was really tactical things. Those are things like having sensors everywhere that tell you where you need to put more solution. We've been doing that. We've been injecting solution to targeted levels, using advanced drilling and targeted drilling. We've been using helicopters to drop drip lines where they couldn't be accessed in the past.

We've been doing a lot of tactical things to what we call get the rocks wet. This new era over the next few years will be about technology, and it'll be about the additive plus the work we're doing to increase temperature of the stockpile. Both of those things coming together is where we see the production curve starting to scale. But we're at the point now where we have to test it and see how it does in the field and what the implications are.

Brian MacArthur: Great. That's very helpful color. Maybe if I could ask another question. I know it's not the core business, but just on the molybdenum. It's come up a number of times in the call. You do have a slide talking about being the world's largest moly producer. How much excess capacity is there in the primary? It's in the U.S., so I would think if you could get it up in these markets, it'd be a good thing to do. My second question is, I see sales go down next year. Is that just byproduct coming out of the U.S. mines or the international mines? Why would it go down next year in a good moly market?

Kathleen Quirk: Yeah. It will vary. We have our two primary mines, and then the production will vary, depending on what the production is from our byproduct mines. We are very positive on the outlook for moly. A lot of the same drivers that we are talking about with copper, the supply challenges at the same time, where uses for molybdenum are growing. So we see it as a real privileged business we have is in being the world's largest producer. We do have some opportunities to expand primary at Climax. So we are kind of dusting off our old schedules of those to look at those. But our priority, Brian, is really to move copper.

But we will continue to look at what the opportunities are so that we can have additional optionality in the moly business.

Brian MacArthur: Great. Thanks very much, Kathleen.

Operator: Our final question will come from the line of Liam Fitzpatrick with Deutsche Bank. Please go ahead.

Liam Fitzpatrick: Good morning, Kathleen and team. Two quick ones, I hope. Firstly, just a clarification on the CapEx guidance for 2027. Does that include anything for Bagdad in terms of the $4.5 billion project number that could be approved later this year? Then the second one is just on buybacks. Based on what you are showing us here on sensitivities at current spot prices, there could be a very material uplift in cash flows next year. So based on that 50% of available cash flows that could be returned to shareholders, how often do you make that decision in terms of how much stock to buy back? Is it on a kind of rolling quarterly basis, or is it less frequent?

Kathleen Quirk: We generally monitor. We have a policy where 50% of the cash flow is for investments in our future growth, and the other half is for shareholder returns. We have the dividends, and then we have been using cash flows above that to get to the 50% to stock. We monitor it quarterly, and look at our, make sure that we're being consistent with the overall distribution policy. You're right. We're going to be generating a lot in the current market environment, and with our growth coming, very significant rise in cash flows. That will, by the policy, mean we have more cash to distribute while at the same time investing in growth.

For the $4.5 billion, we do not have anything in 2027 at this point.

Operator: I'll now turn the call over to management for any closing remarks.

Kathleen Quirk: Well, thank you all for participating. Lots of good questions. I just asked Richard if he wants to make any observations, comments, or closing remarks.

Richard Adkerson: Thanks, Kathleen. You can tell everyone that our management team, our board is very excited about Freeport's outlook and what we have available to us going forward. We appreciate your attention and your support, and if you have any follow-up questions, let us know. Thank you.

Operator: Ladies and gentlemen, that will conclude our call for today. Thank you all for joining. You may now disconnect.
