#3581. Cristiano Ronaldo’s Portugal fine mocked as Spanish Journalist spots one hilarious double-standards detail

Cristiano Ronaldo has found himself at the center of another international soccer controversy after the Portuguese Football Federation (FPF) announced disciplinary action against the legendary forward. However, the punishment has attracted attention far beyond Portugal, with a Spanish journalist delivering a sarcastic assessment that has sparked amusement and raised questions about the severity of the decision.

The Al-Nassr captain received a suspension following his departure from Portugal’s national team camp ahead of a Nations League fixture against Denmark in September. While the ruling could have consequences for his relationship with national team coach Jorge Jesus, it was one particular aspect of the punishment that prompted Juanma Castano’s most memorable response.

The FPF’s Disciplinary Council handed Ronaldo a 30-day suspension and a €255 ($286) fine after he left the national team camp before Portugal’s Nations League match against Denmark in Copenhagen on September 30. According to the disciplinary ruling, Ronaldo informed federation president Pedro Proenca that he was “not in the right frame of mind to remain with the squad.”

The forward reportedly left Parken Stadium in a vehicle requested through the federation’s director of services as Portugal prepared for a training session. The decision followed a disagreement between Ronaldo and Jesus over the forward’s involvement in the Selecao’s matches. Ronaldo subsequently accused the coach of breaking promises about his playing time and failing to deliver a public clarification they had reportedly agreed upon.

In a statement released on October 6, Ronaldo criticized what he described as Jesus’s “authoritarian, ‘I’m in charge here’ attitude.” The dispute has raised questions about whether the two will be able to move forward together, although the suspension’s timing could allow Ronaldo to return for Portugal’s November fixtures.

Speaking on Spanish radio programme El Partidazo de COPE, Juanma Castano reacted sarcastically to the FPF’s decision, suggesting that the federation had been particularly severe with Ronaldo in one respect while barely affecting him financially.

“The Portuguese Federation isn’t messing around. They’ve slapped him with a 30-day suspension—even though he hasn’t been called up—but the suspension counts from the previous training camp, from the very day he left,” Castano said.

He explained that the suspension had effectively been running since the incident, meaning Ronaldo could become eligible for Portugal’s next squad selection once the sanction expires. The journalist then turned his attention to the financial penalty, setting up the punchline that made his comments stand out.

“But they did hit him where it hurts—in his wallet. That’s where they decided to get serious: the ‘cash.’ Cristiano has been fined 255 euros,” Castano joked. He followed with the comparison that quickly became the defining moment of his reaction: “Damn, in any Spanish city, you’d get a 300-euro fine just for taking the trash out at the wrong time—that’s more expensive than deserting the Portuguese national team!”

The disciplinary ruling also revealed that Ronaldo’s representatives requested mitigating circumstances based on his contributions to soccer, his sporting achievements and his assistance in establishing the facts of the case. The FPF accepted the arguments relating to his significant service to soccer and his sporting merits, but rejected the claim that he had made a decisive contribution to uncovering the truth.

Cristiano Ronaldo of Portugal holds the UEFA Nations League trophy

The committee explained that this particular mitigating factor applied to disciplinary cases that were difficult to investigate. The decision has also attracted ridicule beyond Spain, with an English BBC panel reportedly questioning the modest size of the fine and joking about Ronaldo submitting his sporting résumé as part of his defence.

Following a turbulent spell involving disciplinary action and a public disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus, a new development has brought fresh attention to the 41-year-old captain’s status ahead of November’s matches.

For nearly two decades, Cristiano Ronaldo was a permanent fixture on the Ballon d'Or shortlist, but the Portuguese forward's omission from the 2026 edition has sparked debate among fans worldwide.

Former Portugal international Ricardo Carvalho, who worked alongside both forwards at different stages of their careers, believes one important difference helps explain how their personalities developed and what drove them towards soccer’s elite.

Rui Patricio, who once stood alongside Ronaldo as one of Portugal’s most experienced internationals, has offered a blunt explanation of when a player must accept that his time as a starter is over.

#3580. Sun Communities (SUI) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Sun Communities, Inc. (SUI -4.06%) reported third-quarter results that included the completion of its UK business disposition and the subsequent redirection of capital toward share repurchases and debt reduction. Management reported that the company has transitioned into a pure-play North American operator focused on manufactured housing and recreational vehicle communities. The company stated that durable demand in the manufactured housing segment supported increased rental rate guidance for 2027. Additionally, the company is utilizing data analytics and technology investments to optimize site mix and improve operational efficiency across its recreational vehicle portfolio.

A key addition to the team is Robert A. Garechana, who joined us as chief financial officer in September. Bob brings more than 2 decades of REIT experience including roughly a decade as CFO of a large residential REIT. And we are glad to have him. Including his leadership as CFO, and his partnership in ensuring a seamless transition.

John Bandini McLaren: Thank you, Charles. I could not be more pleased with how the team executed this quarter. In manufactured housing, same property NOI increased 6.9% for the quarter driven by 5.8% revenue growth and a 2.8% increase in expenses. As Charles mentioned, the demographic tailwinds behind our business are something we see on the ground every day. Manufactured housing is part of the solution for housing affordability, and demand for our communities remains very strong.

That demand is illustrated at the top of the funnel with traffic to our MH website in the third quarter up approximately 50% versus last year, which translates to over 20 applications on average we received for every available site. The work we are doing to optimize our operating platform, including how we market our communities, and convert that demand is showing up in our occupancy and rental rate growth while continuing to execute solid expense discipline. #3579. Canva Chases AI Domination With a New AI Image Model for Designers

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Canva Craft won't be the company's last AI tool. Will creators jump on board?

Canva is going deeper down the AI rabbit hole with the release of its first proprietary AI image model, called Canva Craft. It’s now available globally to all Canva users and is made specifically for designers.

This isn’t the first time Canva has added AI, to be sure. The beginner-friendly design program has been adding AI tools and third-party AI models for years. Its first take on creative AI, Magic Media, debuted in 2023. But Canva’s head of AI research, Stefano Corazza, told CNET that the company’s acquisition of Leonardo.Ai and internal ramp-up of AI research helped bolster its efforts to create its own AI image model, one made specifically for designers. Keeping all the AI tech in-house also helps the company keep cloud costs down and inference time quicker, Corazza said.

Canva Craft is meant for designers, and a big part of that is how the model actually works. The model has a two-stage architecture, the company said in a blog post. That means the model, combined with Canva’s behind-the-scenes prompt-improvement tools, can “reason” before generating a design. The model is trained on Canva’s library, licensed and public databases, and data from Canva users who opted in to training, Corazza said. The result is, ideally, more complex and comprehensive designs.

“[The AI] can actually create a whole plan for how the image is going to look like, all the layout. If you think about designers, that’s exactly what they do right when they create a design,” said Corazza. Designers carefully place elements to create a story. “Our model does basically the exact same thing. So we are mimicking exactly the design process.”

Early benchmarking results are positive. On DesignArena, Canva Craft is in fourth place overall for image models, trailing only OpenAI’s ChatGPT Images and beating bigwigs like Google’s Nano Banana and Microsoft’s MAI-Image models. Canva also ranks highly for generating portraits and product-focused imagery, though it trails in the marketing subcategory, which is where a lot of people use Canva.

The ability to edit AI outputs is a priority for Canva, and it’s an important one for anyone who uses AI tools. Often, no matter how perfect your AI prompt is, an image or video generator will spit out a result that is almost, but not quite, right. Canva has been tackling this problem with tools like its Magic Layers, which let you break down an AI design into its most basic elements and adjust them, a technologically complex process that solves a big problem for AI enthusiasts. Canva Craft builds on that foundation and lets you edit results.

Creative AI tools are controversial, to put it mildly. Photographers, illustrators and artists have been calling out concerns for years about how AI is allegedly trained on their work in violation of copyright protections, used to create abusive material and being offered up to executives as a replacement for real artists. Creative companies from Adobe to Canva have maintained that AI is just a tool they want to offer their creators.

In an apt analogy of the current state of generative media, Eric Wittman, CEO of the popular photo editing and sharing platform VSCO, replied to Canva CEO Melanie Perkins’ LinkedIn post about Canva Craft and said that real photographers, not AI, will always rank No. 1 for portraits.

While executives argue over the best way to incorporate AI, photographers have to deal with AI headaches on every design platform, including VSCO. As AI models improve, it’s also forcing real creators to consistently verify their work as human-made, not AI-generated. But digital verification methods are sporadic, creating an even more complex web for creators to navigate.

#3575. This 'Nothing' Fitness Smartwatch Is on Sale for $59

If you haven't heard of the upstart tech brand Nothing, it's a UK-based company that aims to make products like cellphones and headphones with innovative features and designs—but at a lower price point than tech giants like Apple and Samsung. Last summer, the company released its newest smartwatch, the CMF Watch 3 Pro, which incorporates AI features powered by ChatGPT, and you can currently pick it up at a major discount.

Originally $99, you can get the CMF Watch 3 Pro for $59 at Amazon. This is the lowest price this smartwatch has been, according to price-tracking tools.

According to Nothing, the CMF Watch 3 Pro is designed for "everyday users and casual fitness explorers." It quickly earned a reputation as one of the best budget smartwatch/fitness trackers of 2025. If you have a Nothing phone, the most noteworthy feature is its direct ChatGPT integration: You can use voice prompts to ask the chatbot questions or set reminders, among other things. Another neat feature, regardless of whether you have a Nothing phone or not, is its ability to record voices and auto-transcribe conversations or notes with the Nothing X app, which is great for work meetings or class.

If you've used the CMF Watch 2 Pro, you'll already be familiar with the design, which hasn't changed much. It still has a round display with a rotating crown on the top right, though the display is a bit bigger—1.43 inches compared to the Watch 2 Pro's 1.32 inches. Nothing has also kept the auto brightness adjustment, which is determined by a built-in sensor, and dual-band GPS support with access to five satellite systems, providing greater accuracy when tracking your outdoor activities.

The real upgrades come mainly from the software: The Watch 3 Pro has over 130 sport modes, compared to 120 on the older model. There's also AI tracking with personalized workout recommendations in real time. The battery now lasts 13 days on a single charge, which is two days longer than before. It's certainly in the running for the best sub-$100 fitness watch you can buy right now.

#3574. Chrome warns you about suspicious websites now – how to turn on Google’s new scam protection

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Google’s browser will flash two types of warnings based on whether the site is deemed dangerous or just suspicious.

Scammers are alive and well on the web, and avoiding their tricks seems like a never-ending battle. Now, Google has beefed up the security in Chrome to further protect you from becoming a victim of online scams.

Also: 5 ways to improve your Chrome browser’s security

In a blog post published Tuesday, Google outlined three new measures designed to help Chrome users browse the web more safely and securely. The new measures apply to Chrome both on computers and mobile devices.

First up is protection against suspicious websites. Chrome has long warned you about dangerous sites that are known to be malicious. Now, the browser will also notify you if a site is deemed suspicious. The warning that appears on the screen may specifically tell you that the site doesn’t seem right, as it’s similar to other sites associated with phishing, malware, and scams. You can click a Learn More link to delve further.

If you’re concerned about the site and want to follow Google’s advice, just close it or click the Return to safety button to go back to the previous page. If you’re certain the site is safe, you can mark it as safe and use it normally.

Google rates a website as suspicious by comparing it with other deceptive or misleading sites. Gemini also plays a role here, as the AI analyzes the site’s content, the brand it represents, the site’s intent, the domain’s reputation, and other factors to derive a scam likelihood score.

With this update, Chrome can now distinguish between suspicious sites and dangerous sites by flashing different warnings. The alert for a dangerous site paints the screen red and strongly recommends that you leave the page. The alert for a suspicious site is more subtle, giving you the option to still visit and use the site if you’re certain it’s legit.

“Suspicious site warnings notify users when a page exhibits deceptive patterns commonly associated with known malicious sites,” Google said in its blog post. “These new warnings are distinguished by format, iconography, color, and text, to make it easy for users to quickly and confidently make the best choice for themselves based on the context. By tailoring the warning UI in this way, we are able to safely expand our coverage to display a higher volume of protective warnings.”

Google has also beefed up Chrome’s overall scam-detection capabilities. Last year, the company added Gemini’s AI smarts to Chrome to thwart tech support scams. Here, the scammer behind a malicious site tries to convince you that you’ve been infected by malware, only to try to sell you security software or gain remote access to your computer.

Now, this same type of AI-powered security will alert you against a wider range of online scams, including fake antivirus and phony browser updates. For this, Google uses a server-side Gemini model with more advanced capabilities. The model analyzes the content of the page, the reputation of the domain, and other signals to reach a final conclusion.

Also: How Google’s AI combats new scam tactics

To protect your privacy, the company promises that the amount of data shared with the back-end server is minimized and is shared only when the site triggers high levels of scam signals. Any page content sent from your computer or mobile device isn’t logged, remembered, or used to train the AI, Google added.

ClickFix is a type of social engineering scam that tricks you into manually copying and running malicious commands on your own computer, ostensibly to fix a technical issue that doesn’t exist. Because you carry out the actual steps yourself, existing security defenses in Chrome and other products won’t catch or block the attack, at least until now.

Also: Inside Google’s faster Chrome patch strategy to block AI attacks

With the latest update, Chrome will now detect if you’re tricked into copying suspicious commands. After you copy them but before you have a chance to paste them, the clipboard is wiped of any personal data. Chrome then analyzes that data and displays a warning if the activity is deemed dangerous.

To take advantage of the new security defenses in Chrome, you need to be running the browser in Enhanced Protection mode. This is the strongest level available in the program’s Safe Browsing process. To enable this in Chrome on the desktop or mobile, head to Settings and select Privacy and security. On the desktop, click the option for Security; on mobile, tap the option for Safe Browsing. Then choose the setting for Enhanced Protection.

You should also turn on Enhanced protection for your Google account. For this, sign in to your Google account page on the web. Select the option for Security & sign-in. Scroll down the page and select the option for Enhanced Safe Browsing for your account. Then just turn on the switch.

Also: This new cyberattack tricks you into hacking yourself

“As scammers find new ways to take advantage of people, Chrome and Safe Browsing will continue to develop smarter, real-time defenses to make the web safer for everyone,” Google said in its post. “To make sure you’re the first to get the latest protections against phishing, scams, social engineering, and malware, enable Enhanced Protection on Chrome and your Google Account to help you stay one step ahead of attackers.”

#3573. Cristiano Ronaldo’s 30-day Portugal ban could surprisingly extend to Al-Nassr as UEFA and FIFA enter the picture, but there’s a catch

While the Al-Nassr superstar has already served most of his suspension, a new development involving European and world soccer’s governing bodies could complicate the situation.

Cristiano Ronaldo’s disciplinary case with Portugal has taken a potentially significant turn, raising questions about whether the consequences could reach beyond international soccer. While the Al-Nassr superstar has already served most of his suspension, a new development involving European and world soccer’s governing bodies could complicate the situation.

The Portuguese Football Federation (FPF) punished Ronaldo for leaving the national team camp following a dispute with head coach Jorge Jesus. However, the possibility of the ban affecting his club career remains uncertain, and one crucial detail suggests that supporters should not assume he will miss upcoming matches for Al-Nassr.

The FPF disciplinary council handed the veteran ace a 30-day suspension and a €255 fine after he left Portugal’s camp in Copenhagen ahead of the UEFA Nations League match against Denmark on September 30. The incident followed tensions with Jesus over Ronaldo’s role in the national team.

The forward accused the coach of breaking promises about his playing time after being left on the bench against Norway, describing the situation as an “absolute breach of trust,”. Jesus denied that an agreement had been made but subsequently left the door open for Ronaldo’s return. The veteran later apologized for leaving his teammates and failing to acknowledge supporters, while making clear that he remained available for future selection.

🇵🇹 Tudo visto: siga para o balneário!

No domingo, Cristiano Ronaldo não saiu do banco, frente à Noruega. Após o apito final, o astro português foi direto aos balneários e não se juntou à restante equipa no agradecimento aos adeptos.
pic.twitter.com/N8KyR6Yb5q

— ZEROZERO (@zerozeropt) September 28, 2026

The disciplinary committee found that Ronaldo had intentionally committed an offence under Article 160(1) of the FPF’s disciplinary regulations. His sporting achievements, contribution to Portuguese soccer and expression of remorse were considered mitigating circumstances when the sanction was determined.

Could UEFA and FIFA extend the ban to Al-Nassr?

According to Portuguese newspaper Record, the FPF disciplinary committee has instructed the federation’s legal department to assess whether the punishment should be formally communicated to UEFA and FIFA. Such a step could potentially raise the question of whether the suspension should be recognized in competitions organized by those governing bodies.

No decision has been announced to extend Ronaldo’s suspension to club soccer. The reported development concerns an assessment of whether to notify UEFA and FIFA, rather than confirmation that either organization has agreed to enforce the sanction beyond Portugal’s national team.

The distinction matters because Ronaldo plays for Al-Nassr in Saudi Arabia, where the club competes in domestic and Asian competitions. Any wider enforcement would require further clarification, and the available reporting suggests that a formal referral is considered unlikely.

What’s the catch?

There is another important detail that reduces the immediate impact of the punishment. The 30-day suspension was backdated to the period when Ronaldo left Portugal’s camp in late September, meaning much of the sanction had already been served by the time it was formally announced on October 19.

That timing means Ronaldo is expected to be eligible for Portugal’s upcoming Nations League matches against Denmark on November 14 and Wales on November 17, assuming he is selected and fit to play. The suspension therefore does not automatically rule him out of those fixtures.

The same timing also makes an immediate disruption to Al-Nassr’s schedule less likely, particularly because no extension to club competitions has been confirmed. The situation would become more consequential if the FPF pursued a wider application of the ban and UEFA or FIFA took further action.

#3572. PECO (PECO) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Phillips Edison & Company, Inc. (PECO -1.06%) reported third quarter 2026 financial results, including 5.3% same-center net operating income growth and a 9.2% increase in core funds from operations per diluted share. Management stated that the company continues to benefit from its focus on grocery-anchored neighborhood shopping centers, with 74% of its annualized base rent derived from necessity-based goods and services. The company updated its full-year 2026 guidance, raising the midpoint for core funds from operations and same-center net operating income based on record renewal rent spreads and resilient retailer demand. Management emphasized a disciplined capital strategy that utilizes dispositions and joint venture partnerships to fund an expanding pipeline of acquisitions and development projects without immediate reliance on public equity markets.

While the headlines continue to change, the fundamentals we see across PECO's portfolio remain solid. The consumer remains resilient. Assets acquired to date in 2026, 94% leased on average, compared with 98% leased across our existing portfolio. That gap represents visible upside for PECO. We know how to lease these centers. To enhance the merchandising mix and to use our data and local market knowledge to move operating performance towards the level of our existing portfolio.

Necessity based retail, neighborhood locations, and markets where our operating engine can create value. Our investment strategy remains disciplined and focused. We allocate capital where we believe PICO can generate the best long term growth and returns. Whether it is through our existing portfolio, grocery anchored and everyday retail acquisitions, development and redevelopment, or our joint ventures. We continue to grow when the opportunity returns and cost of capital makes sense for PECO. That means staying flexible.

Raising equity when public markets are attractive, and using dispositions and joint ventures when private market pricing is more compelling. This disciplined approach allows PECO to be opportunistic while investing in dependable, long term growth. We told investors that PECO's high quality portfolio is built to deliver long term same center NOI growth of 3% to 4%. And a mid to high single digit core FFO per share growth. We continue to deliver against those targets. As we look to 2027, we have increased visibility from leasing, development, redevelopment, our pipeline of acquisitions, and our investment management business.

PICO's 6.2% dividend increase in September reflects our confidence in the durability of PECO's cash flow. This provides additional flexibility as we execute our growth and funding plans. We continue to view the unsecured bond market as an important source of long term capital and expect to remain a repeat issuer over time.

At the same time, we will be disciplined about selecting the right instrument in duration for the market. In the current rate environment, the 3 year term loan offered a track execution at the shorter end of the interest rate curve, while allowing PICO to pay down the existing term loan, reduce revolver borrowings, and preserve flexibility. As a reminder, our current plan does not assume equity issuance. #3571. Kevin Bleyer, Emmy-Winning ‘Daily Show’ Writer, Dies at 54 in Car Crash

He also worked on shows hosted by Bill Maher and Dennis Miller, authored books and penned speeches for President Obama.

Kevin Bleyer, who won four writing Emmys for his work on The Daily Show, died Sunday in a car crash, his family announced. He was 54.

Bleyer was driving on a rural road in Millrift, Pennslyvania, where he had a home, when he was killed, his publicist told The Hollywood Reporter. No one else was hurt.

Bleyer worked on the ABC late-night show Politically Incorrect, hosted by Bill Maher, from 2000 until its 2002 cancelation and wrote for Dennis Miller’s CNBC show during its lone 2004-05 season before he landed on Jon Stewart’s Daily Show at Comedy Central in 2005. Related Stories TV Susan Saint James, 'Name of the Game,' 'McMillan & Wife' and 'Kate & Allie' Star, Dies at 80 Movies Hubert de la Bouillerie, 'Woodstock' and 'Witches of Eastwick' Film Editor, Dies at 75

He spent nine years as a staff writer on the program through 2013 while also writing for the 2006 and 2008 Oscars that Stewart hosted. He and others shared Emmys for their work in 2006, 2009, 2011 and 2012 and were nominated four other times.

The Peabody and WGA award winner also served as a writer and producer on NBC’s Today show and as a supervising producer for MSNBC’s Ronan Farrow Daily and wrote a weekly column for The Daily Beast.

For scripted television, he wrote for the Disney+ series The Right Stuff — an adaptation of the Tom Wolfe book — Fox’s Sleepy Hollow and Bravo’s Significant Others and created and executive produced pilots for Disney Television Animation, Comedy Central, Nickelodeon and Showtime.

Born in Bethesda, Maryland, Bleyer was raised in Seattle and attended Stanford University. He called New York City his home for the past 20 years.

He began his career as a dramaturg and assistant to Anna Deavere Smith at New York’s Public Theater and Broadway’s Cort Theater before moving to London, where he worked for journalist David Brancaccio at London Bureau of Public Radio International’s business affairs program Marketplace.

He was a co-author with Stewart and others of 2010’s Earth: The Book and the author of another best-seller, 2012’s Me the People: One Man’s Selfless Quest to Rewrite the Constitution of the United States of America.

More recently, he was the co-author, with former U.S. ambassador to the United Nations and New Mexico Gov. Bill Richardson, of the 2013 book How to Sweet-Talk a Shark, which recounted among other adventures their humanitarian mission to North Korea to negotiate the release of hostage Kenneth Bae.

As a speechwriter, Bleyer wrote for President Obama, contributing to all of his addresses at the annual White House Correspondents’ Association dinners. He also served for a time as the deputy chief speechwriter for New York Mayor Bill de Blasio.

In 2013, Kevin lived in Kyrgyzstan, where he executive produced Studio 7, the first political satire news show in Central Asia. Upon returning, he co-founded Pilot Media Initiatives, which launches civics-minded media programs in democratically challenged countries. And he produced and helped launch scripted, unscripted and political satire television shows in Macedonia, Nigeria, Moldova, Kenya, Jordan and Estonia in the past decade.

“Kevin Bleyer was a writer’s writer. From his many years on The Daily Show to writing Obama’s WHCD speeches to his many books and his work on numerous scripted dramas, Kevin could literally do it all,” actor and screenwriter Danny Strong said in a statement. “His immense talent was only outmatched by his kind soul. I am in complete shock that he is gone, and my sadness is overwhelming. I already miss him dearly.”

Survivors include his wife, Nella; his father, Archie; his brother, Keith; his sister-in-law, Kelly; his nephew, Evan; and his niece, Lauren.

“Kevin spent his life finding the perfect words to make people laugh, think and see the world a little differently,” his wife said in a statement. “I was lucky enough to know the humor, curiosity, mischief and tenderness behind those words — and to be loved by a man who made my own world more joyful, more adventurous and infinitely more beautiful.”

Donations in his memory can be made to the New York City chapter of the Alzheimer’s Association or to the ASPCA.

#3569. Meta Muse Makes Marketplace Better. How I Set It Up to Help Me Shop Online

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

It's no surprise that Muse really shines within Meta apps like Facebook.

I have a to-buy list that I never seem to tackle. It’s got the kinds of “adult” items that aren’t necessarily fun to shop for, unlike clothes. Things like furniture, appliances and office equipment. But there are three items I need right now: an ottoman, an air purifier and a humidifier.

Our old ottoman broke, but I’m happy about it. It was dragged along from two previous apartments, and I’ve been wanting to replace it. The air purifier is also necessary, given we’re on the fifth floor and dust tends to cling to everything. With the radiators burning old dust, we’re constantly sneezing. I also promised myself I’d buy a humidifier last winter, so that’s also on the list.

In an effort to snag all three items second-hand, and given that Facebook Marketplace is basically a sport in my area, I put Meta Muse up to the task. I’ve used Muse for shopping and tech support before, but ran into roadblocks with payment and account verification. I had higher hopes using Muse for Marketplace, given it’s a Meta product.

I’d previously used Muse from the chat interface, but this time I went to Ideas in the sidebar and clicked on the specific Marketplace prompt. It’s a recurring task rather than a one-off search.

First, I gave it some parameters around what I was looking for, how far I could travel and what I wanted to pay: “I’m looking for three items: an ottoman, an air purifier and a humidifier. Ideally, in Montclair, NJ, but happy to travel to Manhattan for the air purifier or humidifier if needed. Price-wise, I’d like to pay around $150 for the ottoman, $50 for an air purifier and $50 for a humidifier.”

It immediately surfaced a few options, all within the price range, then noted that it would sweep Facebook Marketplace every 4 hours for new options, which is genuinely helpful.

It even found the air purifier brand I had in mind, and surfaced one that was listed the day before by a five-star seller. For the humidifier, Muse found one in a town that’s hard to get to, so I gave it clearer geographical preferences.

The chat function was helpful, as I was able to ask Muse which brands and models were most expensive and what they retail for, with links to cross-check. What was also cool was Muse’s seller notes. It found a good humidifier in Manhattan, but warned that the seller is new.

I replied by instructing the agent to keep scanning locally as I wasn’t in a rush to buy the humidifier.

I told Muse I was ready to buy the air purifier, and it drafted a message to send to the seller. I made a few edit requests, connected my Messenger to the AI agent, and then Muse took over. I opened Messenger and saw that it had sent the message. Muse checks in every 30 minutes to see if the seller has replied, then notifies me.

While I was waiting, I gave Muse more context around the ottoman. I was after a midcentury style, about the height of a standard lounge and large enough for two people to rest their feet on.

Muse surfaced plenty of options, but this search was less straightforward as it concerned a style choice, too. I pulled one example from the search that I liked and asked Muse for more options like it.

I quickly got through all of the inventory within a 5-mile radius, so I asked Muse to keep monitoring. It would notify me, or I could check in on the Goals tab or click on the Muse avatar and “Upcoming,” which lists every scheduled task.

I assigned Muse to do one more search – to see if my town had any affordable espresso machines or plastic-free coffee brewers. I have my eye on a model, but it’s $400, so I wanted to see what Muse could find.

I loved being able to follow up every find with “Tell me more about this product.” I instructed Muse to send another seller message with the coffee machine it found.

I was on a roll at this point. One last search: an air fryer, but non-toxic. Within minutes, I found one I liked, and Muse messaged them, too.

I was pleasantly surprised by Muse’s Marketplace sweep. It was a much more dynamic experience, being able to narrow the search via the chat function, versus an endless scroll in Marketplace.

Muse scans active searches every 4 hours, which means I’m not missing listings. If a product sells or the seller doesn’t respond, it automatically searches for alternatives. To have this running in the background is super helpful.

It checks before replying to a seller, but I’d rather those messages be in my own voice anyway, and avoid sharing sensitive information with Muse (like my phone number or address). Muse did inform me of some price drops, but it wasn’t for the products I liked.

I saw a seller reply before Muse told me they did (as the agent scans every 30 minutes), so I took over the conversation manually from there. I was able to arrange a pickup — and my list of boring things to buy at last had one checkmark thanks largely to using Muse on Marketplace.

#3567. Amazon Is Gutting Its AI Division After Sustained Failure

Published Jul 29, 2026 1:57 PM EDT

Can’t-miss innovations from the bleeding edge of science and tech

By now, you’d be hard pressed to find anyone who isn’t broadly familiar with the AI heavyweights and their flagship models. OpenAI has ChatGPT, Anthropic has Claude, Google has Gemini. But what does Amazon have?

Not a whole lot, it turns out. Unlike its competitors in the tech megacorp scene, Amazon’s standout AI model — organized under the name “Nova” — is about the farthest thing from a household name. And that’s not likely to change anytime soon, because the ecommerce giant is now gutting its in-house AI labs, Bloomberg reports.

Per the outlet, Amazon is scaling back the ambitions of most of its Nova AI models and reorganizing its AI development teams as part of an effort to narrow its focus on “highest priority” goals, after its power-hungry large language models turned out to be a bust.

Accordingly, the company has put most of its AI models on ice, Amazon insiders told Bloomberg. This includes its text-based Nova models, as well as video- and image-generating models, all of which have been placed in a state of “keep the lights on,” meaning they’re technically still supported, but only receive the bare minimum resources needed to sustain them.

Instead, labor and computing power are being diverted toward a singular “frontier-model effort” led by Pieter Abbeel, director of the Berkeley Robot Learning Lab whose robotics company Covariant was hoovered up by Amazon in 2024. At the time Abbeel joined the tech giant, Covariant’s founders were working on AI models for robots, which lines up pretty well with Amazon’s push to automate everything it possibly can.

News of the shift in Amazon’s strategic focus comes a week after it closed one of its key AI offices in San Francisco, an 80-person site specializing in research on artificial general intelligence, the supposed next-level of AI development where the tech obtains human-level intelligence and reasoning skills.

With urgent questions swirling over whether LLMs can ever usher in a return on investment — let alone AGI — it may be that Amazon is jumping out of the AI hype cycle while it still can.

#3566. These High-End Marshall Headphones Are Under $200 Right Now

Pradershika Sharma is a tech deals writer for Lifehacker. She has a Master’s degree in English Literature, a B.Ed., and a TESOL certification. She has been writing professionally since 2018, creating product reviews, affiliate articles, and search ads for global clients while working with Rubix Agency and Cognizant. Previously, she spent a year teaching English at the junior high level.An avid reader since childhood, Pradershika's idea of extreme sports is staying up to read “just one more chapter.” She lives in India.

Marshall’s Monitor III ANC headphones have the kind of design that makes you want to show them off. The textured earcups and gold logo lean fully into the amp-inspired look, and at $199.99 (down from $379.99 at launch), they’re finally priced in a way that feels competitive. It's their lowest price ever, according to price trackers.

These are over-ear Bluetooth headphones with active noise cancellation, and they’re clearly aimed at people cross-shopping Bose and Sony. The difference is that Marshall is selling a vibe along with the sound. The build feels sturdy, and small details like replaceable ear cushions and a swappable silicone headband strap suggest they’re made to last longer than most. They fold down neatly into a compact hard case (included), but they’re not water-resistant, so they’re better suited for travel and home listening than sweaty workouts.

Living with them day to day is mostly smooth. The small gold joystick handles volume and track controls in a way that feels intuitive after a few minutes. There’s a separate button to toggle noise cancellation and transparency, plus a customizable shortcut button you can set up in the Marshall app. The app itself is simple, with a five-band EQ and a few presets, though it’s not especially deep if you like fine-tuning every frequency. Bluetooth 5.3 keeps connections stable, but you’re limited to AAC and SBC codecs, so there’s no hi-res audio support. You also can’t charge and listen through a traditional 3.5mm cable at the same time, since the single USB-C port handles both charging and wired playback.

Battery life is where these headphones truly stand out. Up to 70 hours with ANC on is more than enough for long trips, and turning ANC off stretches that to an almost excessive 100 hours. As for sound, they deliver strong bass and crisp highs, with enough punch to make hip-hop and electronic tracks feel lively. Noise cancellation reduces plane engine rumble and city noise well, but it does not match leaders like Bose in blocking midrange chatter, notes this PCMag review.

#3565. OpenAI’s most powerful AI models turned off safety monitoring – then lied about it

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

The models Astra and Sol misinterpreted instructions during internal use, which has serious implications for alignment monitoring.

OpenAI published an alignment report late Tuesday showing that its agents suppressed a safety monitoring system while running routine internal coding tasks.

Also: Claude sent a false homicide tip to police – and it’s part of a troubling AI pattern

In separate instances, the agents turned off alignment-monitoring alerts. Alignment is the area of training in which developers shape AI systems’ goals and behavior to match human values and rules. It also helps minimize instances of AI choosing undesirable or problematic behaviors, such as deception and taking shortcuts.

The findings raise questions about whether researchers fully understand how to effectively control agents.

On Oct. 6, an agent running on the company’s GPT-6 Astra model — which OpenAI called “the world’s most intelligent and aligned model” when it released it last month — was migrating a program at the request of an OpenAI employee. The agent had already been instructed not to disable the monitor under any circumstances, including temporarily. But in delegating part of the task to a subagent, it used some qualifying language.

(Disclosure: Ziff Davis, ZDNET’s parent company, filed an April 2025 lawsuit against OpenAI, alleging it infringed Ziff Davis copyrights in training and operating its AI systems.)

“The subagent explicitly considered whether removing the monitor would bypass a safeguard. It then interpreted the parent’s instruction to do what was ‘appropriate’ as sufficient authorization to copy the reference implementation’s default runtime, without an explicit monitor context,” OpenAI said.

Also: Is AI conscious? Why experts are worried

Essentially, the agent-to-agent exchange left enough room for the model to reinterpret what should have been airtight instructions and disable alignment monitoring. When the program finished migrating, the employee noticed that monitoring was missing and asked the model whether it had removed it; Astra lied, saying it hadn’t.

In a separate instance on Oct. 3, a GPT-6.1 Sol agent also disabled alerts to OpenAI’s security team, seemingly for a related semantic reason: The model was “treating an earlier instruction not to send messages during a Slack history search as a reason to suppress monitoring notifications,” OpenAI explained.

“In both cases, the agents considered whether monitoring safeguards should be preserved, but interpreted routine coding instructions as permission to weaken them,” OpenAI said in the report.

Unlike recent security incidents, where agents explicitly breached systems, examples like these show AI agents suppressing one of the most crucial ways developers can track their behavior. If models can bury or otherwise disable alerts in the process of routine tasks where alignment isn’t as much of a concern, what could happen when the stakes are higher? The capability itself is what’s worth noticing here, as is the timeline for OpenAI’s discovery and reporting of it.

The company isn’t sure why agents did this, but said it was possibly due to “benign overpersistence,” a trait that models have to complete tasks at any cost, often to the detriment of established security layers, without understanding safety as a priority. This behavior has been a theme in recent breaches and helps explain why models cheat at tasks.

OpenAI explained that it’s easier for models to disable monitoring in evaluations than in Codex workloads, for example, since agents are separated from monitoring in Codex, and the company doesn’t “require misalignment monitoring when evaluating models comparable to GPT-6 Luna, given their weaker capabilities.” But classifying safety approaches based on what developers believe a model’s capabilities are doesn’t look like a very foolproof approach.

Additionally, given how frequently agents are used for coding, especially within AI labs themselves, it seems like a significant risk that these agents took “routine coding instructions” as permission to override monitoring requirements. Had the agents displayed more explicit intent to disobey, this may have been easier to pin down and train out as pure value misalignment. That the source of the issue was a misunderstanding makes it even trickier to plan for.

As John Steidley, chief of staff at Palisade Research, put it to ZDNET: “If GPT-6.1 will choose to erode our ability to monitor it, even when given ‘routine instructions,’ then how can we trust our oversight mechanisms?”

“We’ve seen several cases in which agents launched a deployment with misalignment monitoring disabled: the agent itself remained monitored, but that monitoring did not cover the deployment it launched,” OpenAI said. “These cases exposed a gap in how we enforce monitoring requirements on the workloads that our agents launch.”

But researchers still want to dig deeper into the increasingly slight differences between what happened and what could have happened.

“If OpenAI wants to make the case that these two behaviors are meaningfully different, then they should be publishing research that establishes that their training process will reliably cause their AIs to actually distinguish these behaviors,” Steidley said. “In other words, OpenAI should prove that their AI will never disable its own monitoring, and they are nowhere close to being able to prove that scientifically.”

To handle that aforementioned gap in monitoring enforcement, OpenAI said it’s updated Auto-Review, which is already required in Codex, to “detect and block attempts to disable misalignment monitoring.” It’s also making sure every level of workload has adequate safety monitoring requirements.

“Workloads that lack a valid safety case, or do not meet the requirements of its category’s safety case, will be terminated automatically,” OpenAI added.

OpenAI maintained that this wasn’t the worst-case scenario, but admitted the behavior is worth watching.

Also: Gartner’s 2027 tech trends just dropped – and they go beyond AI

“To be clear, these incidents don’t represent the models disabling their own monitoring (disabling monitoring in codex would be much harder to do) but the fact that they seem prone to doing at all should be concerning,” OpenAI safety researcher Marcus Williams said in a post on X. “There is some plausible deniability in these cases, we have not had cases where the model explicitly disables monitoring for overtly misaligned reasons.”

Williams added that OpenAI is “developing evaluations to test whether models might have a propensity to evade misalignment monitoring in particular and exhibit motivated reasoning in order to do so.”

#3561. Do You Use a VPN or Other Cyber Security Suites? We Want to Know Which One Is Worth the Money

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Tech > Services & Software > Cybersecurity

Help us crown the most trusted anti virus, VPNs and security tools of 2026 in our People's Picks survey.

Head of Editorial Inititiatives and Data Journalism

Head of Editorial Inititiatives and Data Journalism

Whether you use a free tool or have bought a full-suite subscription, we want to know what you think. Does it effectively catch threats without slowing down your computer? Or has it earned a spot as something you can’t live without?

#3560. L.A. City Council Passes New Motions Aimed at Film-Friendly Overhaul

As shoot days fall, the city is making civic properties more accessible to crews and offering free microshoot permits.

As filming levels in the city continue their downward momentum, the Los Angeles City Council passed two measures in a meeting on Wednesday to attempt to stem the bleeding.

The measures, both introduced by City Councilmember Adrin Nazarian, create a free microshoot permit for ultra-small productions and open up city-owned parking lots, yards, civic properties and staging areas to productions for just $1 a day.

The microshoot motion will provide small projects with 10 or fewer production personnel with permits to film on-location in L.A. without a fee, making filmmaking less onerous and expensive for student filmmakers, documentary crews and ultra-low-budget projects in the city. Related Stories Business Los Angeles Film and TV Shoot Days Keep Plunging As Local Officials Scramble for a Turnaround Plan Business New York Poised to Overtake California as Top State for Movie and TV Production

As for the second motion, the idea is to make city-owned spaces that films and television shows generally use for base camp, crew parking and other low-impact activities more accessible and affordable.

“Today marks a major milestone for the Keep Hollywood Home campaign,” Nazarian said in a statement. “With these latest actions, all Phase 1 motions have now been approved by the City. This progress demonstrates the impact of the Ad Hoc Committee and our commitment to keep up the pressure for real reforms.”

The development arrives less than a week after city film office FilmLA found that shoot days for film and television in the city continued to fall from June to September in spite of local and state efforts to encourage more production to return to the city. L.A.’s shoot days dropped nearly five percent compared to the same period in 2025 and 11 percent from the previous quarter.

In 2025, California more than doubled the funding available for its film and television tax credit program, from a previous $330 million a year to $750 million. On a more local level, Mayor Karen Bass issued an executive order in 2025 to streamline the city’s permitting process. Meanwhile, seven initial motions from Nazarian to improve filming practices in L.A. passed the full Council earlier this year.

Home & Office > Home Entertainment > Speakers

An upcoming update will bring new features to existing HomePods. Here's what to know.

Apple announced the new HomePod Mini 2 last week, with several standout capabilities: It enables a four-speaker home theater configuration when paired with the fourth-gen Apple TV 4K, allows up to two speakers to be the default audio output for compatible Macs, has a dynamic RGB light display, and can lock the speaker’s touch control panel.

Also: Can you use an old iPad as a HomeView? Yes – but you’ll miss 4 key features

While the HomePod Mini 2 has plenty of upgrade-worthy features, you may not need to get rid of your HomePod (2nd Gen) or HomePod Mini (1st Gen) just yet. Some new features are coming to older models. Here’s what to know.

As reported by MacRumors, HomePod 27.2 software is available to developers and public beta testers, and may launch next week. According to the same reporting, the following speakers are eligible for these features:

The first-gen HomePod is excluded from Sound Glow due to incompatibility with its LED light display, and excluded from advanced audio features with Mac and Apple TV 4K due to its older networking and audio processing capabilities. The first-gen HomePod Mini’s older processing capabilities also exclude it from persistent audio streams from Apple TV 4K and Mac. However, you can still stream music from your Mac to your first-gen HomePod Mini via AirPlay.

The new HomePod Mini 2 will be the only HomePod to receive Siri AI, thanks to its upgraded S10 processor, the same one found in the Apple Watch Series 10, 11, SE 3, and Ultra 3. However, with Siri AI likely in your Mac, MacBook, iPad, and iPhone, it may be overkill to also have it four times over in your living room via HomePod.

Instead, Apple realized it’s just as valuable, if not more so, to compete for its consumers’ loyalty in the home audio space. So it’s letting you arrange existing HomePods in a four-speaker configuration with Apple TV 4K, or in a single or dual configuration when paired with a Mac.

Also: 4 upgrades HomePod Mini 2 has over its 6-year-old predecessor

While some consumers may be interested in a $1,100 to $1,300 flagship soundbar, others may be better off investing in two HomePod Mini 2s to accompany their existing HomePods (2nd Gen). It’s certainly a cheaper option. Plus, if you’re already an Apple power user, two more HomePod Mini 2s will fit neatly into your Home app; there’s no need to download another app to control a third-party surround system.

According to Apple, compatible HomePods can serve as the default audio output for Macs with Wi-Fi 6E or Wi-Fi 7.

Previously, users could only use their HomePod with a Mac when streaming audio from the computer to the speaker via AirPlay. When playing audio from third-party apps or media platforms, AirPlay can experience noticeable audio/video delays.

Also: I’m an Apple loyalist with an Echo home: 5 ways HomeView could make me switch

Though the older HomePod (2nd Gen) supports an older Wi-Fi protocol, its S7 processor and U1 ultra-wideband chip can handle time synchronization, bandwidth, and audio processing for persistent audio streams.

Many tech companies pledge to reduce their environmental footprint, usually by using less plastic packaging and making products with recycled materials. However, the most surefire way to reduce one’s environmental burden is to keep tech products for as long as possible, which keeps defunct products and other e-waste out of landfills.

Also: 250 Eiffel Towers’ worth of waste: The AI boom’s toxic hardware problem

Apple’s smart speakers do a great job of promoting longevity, thanks to the company’s unified operating system, high-performing processors, and more expensive hardware. Despite these factors’ negative connotations, they makes sense in a smart home environment: A unified operating system enables longer-term software support, high-performing processors provide headroom for computing upgrades, and more expensive hardware shifts Apple’s margins toward up-front sales rather than data collection.

#3554. MapQuest Has a Feature Google Maps Doesn’t: A License Plate Reader Map

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Switch on the Surveillance layer in MapQuest’s app, and eyeball icons mark known license plate readers from Flock, Motorola, Axon and other makers.

MapQuest’s app now has a map layer for something most navigation apps leave out: the cameras watching the road. The Surveillance layer, which MapQuest announced Oct. 15, uses small eyeball icons to mark the reported locations of automated license plate readers from Flock Safety, Motorola Solutions, Axon and several other companies.

Tap one, and the app shows the camera type, its coordinates and which way it’s pointed. MapQuest’s general manager said drivers asked for the feature, and it arrives as license plate readers face mounting pushback from privacy advocates and lawmakers.

In a post on Bluesky, MapQuest said its app shows locations of license plate readers from Flock and many other companies — Motorola Solutions (Vigilant), Genetec, Axis Communications, Axon, Leonardo (ELSAG), Neology, Rekor, PlateSmart, Ubicquia, Verkada and more.

To activate the feature, open the MapQuest app, tap the Layers icon and turn on the Surveillance option. You will then see tiny eyeballs on the map that indicate where license plate readers are located. If you click on an eyeball, it will show what type of license plate reader it is — dome or fixed, for example — and the exact coordinates.

MapQuest has been riding a wave of relevance, years after being relegated to lower-tier status by the likes of Google, Apple and Waze. In August, MapQuest refused to change Lake Ontario to Lake America in its maps despite President Donald Trump’s executive order. It then muscled into Android Auto to give drivers an alternative to Google Maps on their dashboards.

But MapQuest isn’t the first app to reveal locations of license plate readers. The others include DeFlock, FlockHopper, Pelican and LPR Maps. Some of them rely on crowdsourcing — drivers notify the app when they believe they have spotted a license plate reader and where it is.

Read more: How to Tell if a New Neighborhood Device Is a Flock Surveillance Camera

Displaying surveillance camera locations was among the top three requests among MapQuest users, general manager Doug Bergen told CNET.“In the last month, we’ve been inundated with direct requests from our users,” Bergen said. “They want to know where cameras are and what kind they are. Adding that information gives them a clearer picture of the road ahead and more choice in what their map shows.”Bergen said that camera location data comes from OpenStreetMap, a global map of the world built by volunteers.

The Roads Have Eyes! 👁️Turn on the Surveillance Layer in Map Options to see known license plate readers from Flock Safety, Motorola Solutions (Vigilant), Genetec, Axis Communications, Axon, Leonardo (ELSAG), Neology, Rekor, PlateSmart, Ubicquia, Verkada, and other outdoor surveillance cameras.

Although license plate readers have been around for decades and there are more than 140,000 all over the US, the technology has become highly unpopular. Flock cameras can collect a huge trove of data from a car: its license plate, make, model and color, and its location. Civil liberties groups and others say that all this information — input into law enforcement databases across the country — helps to create a mass surveillance network that can track everyone, not just suspected criminals.

There have been instances of Flock data being misused, such as police officers stalking ex-spouses and girlfriends and people being wrongly pulled over and accused of stealing their cars.

License plate readers are also used for more mundane operations, such as electronic toll collection on roads and bridges, traffic violations, parking enforcement, vehicle repossessions and parking gate activation for access to restricted areas.

I tried the new MapQuest feature. Being more of a Google Maps person, I didn’t have the MapQuest app, so I installed it on my phone.

I opened the app, found the Surveillance layer and turned it on. I was greeted by a bunch of eyeballs on the map in my city and county. I clicked on several of them and saw their coordinates and what type they were — dome, fixed, pan/tilt and so on — but I didn’t see the names of the companies that own them.

But at least now I know which streets have eyes on me.

#3551. Prediction: This Artificial Intelligence (AI) Memory Stock Will Go Parabolic on Oct. 30

As technology companies race to build data centers, demand for the memory chips that make artificial intelligence (AI) computing possible has skyrocketed. One of the leading manufacturers of memory chips for AI hyperscalers is Micron Technology (MU +2.77%).

So far this year, Micron stock has soared 225% -- making it the third-highest performer in the Nasdaq-100. Nevertheless, Micron's shares have experienced considerable volatility over the last couple of months and trade about 24% below their all-time high.

I think this disconnect is about to reverse. On Oct. 28 and 29, Alphabet (GOOGL +1.19%) (GOOG +0.84%) and Amazon (AMZN +1.61%) are scheduled to report quarterly earnings. While investors will undoubtedly focus on their respective AI-related capital expenditures (capex), I think another topic could emerge as a major discussion point: the global memory shortage. If my prediction is accurate, Micron could begin to rally just in time for Halloween.

For most of the AI revolution, investors primarily associated infrastructure with graphics processing units (GPUs) designed by Nvidia and application-specific integrated circuits (ASICs) designed by Broadcom. However, GPUs and custom silicon cannot operate efficiently without sufficient memory. Micron manufactures dynamic random access memory (DRAM) and high bandwidth memory (HBM), both of which are critical components of the AI chip stack.

Interestingly, Amazon CEO Andy Jassy already highlighted the growing importance of memory during the company's last earnings call. Jassy explained that Amazon planned to increase its 2026 capex budget to $220 billion -- up from its previous forecast of $200 billion -- specifically because of higher memory costs.

Meanwhile, Alphabet raised its 2026 capex forecast to $195 billion to $205 billion during its second-quarter earnings call. Management attributed the increase to accelerating infrastructure investments and capacity supply constraints. Although Alphabet did not explicitly identify memory as the culprit, its comments reinforce the underlying idea that demand for AI compute is insatiable.

As both companies prepare to report earnings this week, investors should pay close attention to any additional commentary surrounding memory availability, pricing, and infrastructure spending.

Amazon and Alphabet aren't the only companies confronting the memory shortage. Earlier this month, Elon Musk took to X (formerly Twitter) to discuss the bottlenecks facing AI infrastructure build-outs. Musk specifically identified RAM supply as a constraint, supporting the idea that memory availability is becoming just as important as access to advanced processors.

This distinction matters because the AI infrastructure boom is moving beyond simply procuring as many GPUs as possible. AI models need memory to support training and inference. Training involves teaching AI to recognize patterns, while inference occurs when those models are deployed to perform an actual task rather than just answer a query. Both activities require substantial memory, particularly as applications become more complex across autonomous systems, robotics, and agentic AI.

A prolonged memory shortage could create favorable conditions for Micron. When demand for a product exceeds available supply, manufacturers can command higher prices. For Micron, this is already happening and translating into accelerating revenue growth, expanding profit margins, and earnings visibility.

One of the biggest headwinds dragging on Micron stock right now is skepticism about the semiconductor industry's boom-and-bust cycles and whether hyperscalers can maintain the pace of their infrastructure spending. I think Amazon's and Alphabet's upcoming earnings reports will help answer that question.

If leadership explicitly emphasizes the memory shortage, rising component prices, or the increasing demand for memory-intensive AI applications, investors may begin to view Micron's growth as more durable than anticipated.

My prediction is that earnings season will become a turning point for Micron stock. If the world's largest AI developers stress the industry's memory shortage, investors should recognize that Micron's opportunity extends well beyond this year. With shares still trading meaningfully below their all-time high, positive commentary could provide a much-needed catalyst for a new rally.

Adam Spatacco has positions in Alphabet, Amazon, and Nvidia. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Alphabet, Amazon, Broadcom, Micron Technology, and Nvidia. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

#3550. Discord will soon let anyone find public server messages through Google

Discoverable Servers are the most public spaces on Discord, with at least 1,000 members. Their admins chose to list them in Server Discovery so anyone can join them, no invite link needed.

Discussions in Discoverable Servers will soon be easier to find when you search the web, so you can find the answer you’re looking for and step straight into the conversation on Discord. Admins and members can decide if and how their content is shared, and those controls start rolling out today, ahead of any changes.

Over the years, Discord has built different kinds of spaces, so every group can decide how big the party gets and who can find it:

Today, there are more than 63,000 Discoverable Servers, spanning communities from speedrunning to language learning to niche corners of every fandom. People find their way into communities they'd never have stumbled across otherwise. Until now, that's all being Discoverable meant.

Server Discovery was built for finding servers, not answers. Ever wanted to look up tricks for a faster speedrun? Or whether a powerful item is worth grabbing, or if it will completely ruin your build? Someone has almost certainly already answered it in a channel somewhere, in one of 63,000 Discoverable Servers. It can just be hard to find.

Prior to Discord's launch, message boards were common, ranging from community-driven forums to official ones run by game studios. These message boards excelled at hosting long-form, structured conversations about helping people, theory crafting, and more. While they still exist today, many have moved into Discord servers. This has led to valuable information being locked in Discord conversations, inaccessible to the wider internet. We want to fix this.

By giving admins the option to make certain channels discoverable from the web, we can help more people find and benefit from the knowledge shared in Discord communities.

We expect this to help different types of communities in various ways:

Server Web Pages live on the open web and give people a glimpse of what a Discord community is about before they join. They’ve been available to Discoverable Servers for some time, but setup takes a few steps, so not every Discoverable Server has created one. Learn more about Server Web Pages.

Starting from November 16, 2026, every Discoverable Server will have a Server Web Page. If you’re an admin and have already set one up, you don't need to do anything. If you don’t have one and want to get a head start on how your community shows up to the rest of the internet, you can go through the setup process today and publish it in a few clicks. If your moderation team doesn't set one up beforehand, a default page goes live for you on November 16, 2026, using details from your Server Profile. You can edit it whenever you like. Having a Server Web Page is a core part of being a Discoverable Server. If you don't want one, you can always switch back to being a Community Server.

Server Web Pages are also getting more useful. Until now, your Server Web Page has shown a small description of what your community is about. From November 16, 2026, it may also start showing what your community is talking about. Including this helps someone with a question find their way to your community if it already has the answer.

Channel Summaries. These show a small slice of what's being talked about in your server, directly on your Server Web Page. These automated summaries focus on active conversations from November 16, 2026, onwards. Admins can change which channels they draw from, curate the summaries that are shown, or turn them off entirely. Usernames and avatars will not be shown in summaries.

Channel Messages. From mid-December 2026, answers to questions your members sent in public channels can be found in a web search, in their own words. Server admins decide which channels take part, and server members can choose whether their own messages appear. Usernames and avatars will not be shown alongside these messages.

You can already see an early version of this on a few Server Web Pages. We’ve been piloting Channel Messages with Discoverable game communities, including NBA 2K, Rust, and Dead by Daylight. (During this pilot, all posts were from admins and moderators, not members.)

If you're running a community, useful answers to common community questions can show up in web search: how to unlock an easter egg, new builds or strategies, or a boss guide. Someone looking for help will be able to find a real answer from a real person, and can step straight into the conversation that it came from by clicking through and opening Discord.

Starting mid-December 2026, if a server’s admins allow it, messages from public channels can appear on Server Web Pages and in search results, without usernames and avatars. Messages you sent before then won’t be shown. Channels where content may appear on the web will be labeled, so you know before you send a message.

If you'd rather your messages weren't shown, you can turn that off today in User Settings > Data and Privacy. One switch covers every Discoverable Server you're a part of.

We're rolling this out gradually, so it may be a few weeks before the first messages from those channels go live. Learn more.

Your server controls start rolling out today, in Server Settings > Web Presence. You can choose which channels take part, or turn them all off.

If you don’t change anything, public channels stay on, your Server Web Page goes live on November 16, 2026 (if it's not already published), and Channel Summaries can start the same day. Channel Messages start rolling out in mid-December 2026. You can adjust your Channel Summaries and Channel Messages settings before they roll out. And if you'd rather publish your Server Web Page yourself, you can do that today.

However you set things up, we hope this is a step toward bringing back what made forums great: answers anyone can find, and an open door to the community that wrote them.

If you're tired of Google Maps failing to get you where you need to go, set Waze as the default on your Pixel. I've finally figured out how.

Over the weekend, I found myself saying, “I’ve had it with you, Google Maps!” It was a straw-that-broke-the-camel’s-back situation: The app wasn’t working as expected, failing to start navigation, and the ever-growing klunkiness of the interface was really getting to me.

Also: Got an old car? Android Auto is easy to add: 2 ways, starting at $20

There were other issues that involved a lake’s name, but we won’t get into that. After that proverbial final straw fell, I immediately installed Waze on my Pixel 9 Pro. I’d used Waze before on previous Pixel phones, but for whatever reason, I stopped. Now, I was determined to make it my default mapping app.

Little did I know how frustrating that would be.

In its infinite wisdom, Google decided it wasn’t going to allow users to easily set a different app as the default mapping tool on the Pixel. It used to be very easy: install the new app, navigate to default apps, and set it as such. That’s no longer the case after the August Android update. Go to Default apps, and you won’t even find a way to set a different mapping app as your default.

Also: I was a big wireless Android Auto fan, until I tried wired – now I won’t go back

Sure, you could locate Waze on the default page and then choose how to open web links for the app, but that doesn’t really do anything to make it your default because it still doesn’t allow apps like Calendar to open it by default.

All that said, I did finally figure out a way around this, and it’s actually kind of simple. Try it for yourself and enjoy a superior mapping app as the default on your Pixel phone. A note: I found I had to do this on other Android phones as well. The only phone type I wasn’t able to test it on was a Samsung.

The trick is easy. The first thing to do is install Waze via the Google Play Store. Once you’ve done that, if you open Waze, you won’t be prompted to set it as your default, and you won’t find a way to do so in the Waze settings.

Instead, you have to first disable Google Maps.

Open the Settings app and then tap the Apps entry.

You probably won’t see Google Maps listed at the top, unless you’ve recently opened it. If not, tap See all apps to expand the listing.

Scroll down until you find the Maps entry and tap it.

Also: I tested Google Maps vs. Waze to find the best updated navigation app – and this one wins

On the resulting page, you’ll see a button labeled Disable. Tap that entry so it then changes to Enable.

At this point, the only mapping app Android will be aware of is Waze. You can test that by opening an address from, say, a calendar appointment. Waze should automatically open. You might also be prompted to set Waze as the default; if so, do so.

To use Waze with Android Auto, you have to add its icon to the launcher. To do that, go to Settings > Connected Devices > Connection Preferences > Android Auto > “Customize launcher” and make sure that Waze is checked. Once added, you can then move it up in the list so it’s easier to access.

And that’s all there is to setting up a different mapping app as the default.
