#3523. Armstrong (AWI) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Armstrong World Industries, Inc. (AWI -5.74%) reported record third-quarter net sales and double-digit growth in adjusted EBITDA, supported by strong demand in the Architectural Specialties segment and disciplined pricing in Mineral Fiber. Management updated its full-year revenue outlook to reflect a stronger specialties performance but adjusted the adjusted EBITDA midpoint lower to account for accelerating freight and raw material costs. The company remains focused on its digital growth platforms and expanding its presence in the high-growth data center vertical. Capital allocation continues to prioritize shareholder returns through a 10% dividend increase and active share repurchases, while maintaining a healthy balance sheet with ample liquidity for further strategic acquisitions.

Operator: Hello, and thank you for standing by. My name is Marina, and I will be your conference operator today. At this time, I would like to welcome everyone to the Armstrong World Industries third quarter 2026 earnings call. All lines have been placed on mute to prevent any background noise. After the speaker's remarks, there will be a question and answer session. I would now like to turn the conference over to Theresa Womble, Vice President, Investor Relations and Corporate Communications. Please go ahead.

Theresa Womble: Thank you, and welcome everyone to our call this morning. On today's call, Mark Hershey, our CEO, and Chris Calzaretta, our CFO, will discuss Armstrong World Industries' third quarter 2026 results and rest of year outlook. We have provided a presentation to accompany these results that is available on the investors section of the Armstrong World Industries website. Our discussion of operating and financial performance includes non-GAAP financial measures within the meaning of SEC Regulation G. A reconciliation of these measures with the most directly comparable GAAP measures is included in the earnings press release and in the appendix of the presentation we issued this morning. Both are available on our investor relations website.

During this call, we will be making forward-looking statements that represent the view we have of our financial and operational performance and our expectations as of today's date, October 27, 2026. These statements involve risks and uncertainties that may differ materially from those expected or implied. We provide a detailed discussion of these risks and uncertainties in our SEC filings, including those related to market conditions, inflationary pressures, and tariffs. We undertake no obligation to update any forward-looking statement beyond what is required by applicable securities law. Now, I will turn the call over to Mark.

Mark Hershey: Thank you, Theresa. Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining us. We delivered another solid quarter at Armstrong, demonstrating the resilience of our businesses and continued progress in our growth platforms. I was particularly impressed by how our people, across a broad range of roles and responsibilities, united to respond to a rapidly changing cost environment while continuing to serve our customers and advance our growth initiatives. In the quarter, we delivered record third quarter sales and double-digit growth in both adjusted EBITDA and adjusted diluted EPS. Net sales increased 12%, adjusted EBITDA increased 11%, and adjusted diluted EPS increased 10%.

This is strong revenue growth against a largely flat overall market, and importantly, both of our operating segments contributed to that performance and expanded adjusted EBITDA margins year-over-year. I believe these results demonstrate two things clearly: Our growth strategy is working, and the underlying value creation model that has distinguished Armstrong for many years remains firmly intact. Mineral Fiber delivered solid AUV and positive volume growth, while Architectural Specialties produced exceptional organic growth across a broad range of offerings and product categories. At the same time, input costs accelerated rapidly during the quarter, particularly freight and raw materials.

We also saw timing-related mix pressure in Mineral Fiber, as can occur from time to time in some of our lower AUV channels. Together, those factors dampened the margin expansion we would otherwise have expected from this level of revenue growth. Accordingly, we responded to the recent cost headwinds with the same discipline that has served Armstrong well through previous inflationary cycles: enhanced productivity, swift spending controls, optimized transportation, and market-based pricing. Some of those benefits take time to flow through, particularly in our custom project-based Architectural Specialties businesses. We expect the impact of these dynamics to moderate as timing-related mix pressure normalizes and our mitigating actions take fuller effect. Our updated guidance now reflects these impacts.

Taken together, the quarter showcases the resilience of our businesses, underlying strength of demand, and continued traction of our strategic growth initiatives as we enter the final quarter of the year. Turning first to Mineral Fiber. Net sales increased 7%. AUV was the primary contributor to growth, driven principally by price together with a positive volume contribution. Notably, this was our third consecutive quarter of Mineral Fiber volume growth and the fifth of the past six quarters. While demand for our higher value WAVE products remained healthy and again outpaced other product categories, volume was particularly strong in several of our lower AUV channels. Most notably in Canada, where customers accelerated purchases ahead of anticipated tariff actions affecting grid pricing.

Because we sell a complete ceiling system, that grid activity in Canada also pulled through additional Mineral Fiber volume. We benefited from that timing-related grid activity in the quarter, as well as from strong data center growth to deliver a solid earnings performance, while at the same time responding appropriately to continued steel and tariff cost headwinds. Overall, we view the lower AUV channel strength in the quarter as a timing-related mix dynamic that reduced the margin benefit we would normally expect from this level of sales growth.

That said, even with the unusual mix dynamic and the swift rise in freight and raw material costs, Mineral Fiber delivered 8% adjusted EBITDA growth and expanded adjusted EBITDA margin by 40 basis points to 44%. I want to emphasize that point. In the face of the highest input cost inflation we've experienced since 2022, Mineral Fiber delivered a 44% adjusted EBITDA margin and expanded margin year over year. That is strong evidence of the durability of this business and the ability of our teams to respond to escalating inflation through productivity, cost controls, and other near-term mitigation actions.

Our Mineral Fiber manufacturing teams were part of that effort and continued to execute well this quarter, setting an all-time record in plant reliability for the network and topping their record Q2 performance, while also keeping our perfect order fulfillment measure at high levels. Plant reliability, product quality, and dependable customer service are key enablers of our commercial performance and meaningful differentiators for Armstrong in the market, as we strive to support our loyal distributor and contractor customers with exceptional quality and service. Turning to Architectural Specialties, net sales increased 22%, including impressive organic growth of 16%, the highest quarterly organic growth rate in the segment's history.

Growth was broad-based across the AS portfolio, supported by multiple consecutive quarters of healthy demand and strong project activity in transportation, healthcare, education, and other commercial applications. I continue to be encouraged by our quoting and order intake rates and by the ability of our commercial teams to drive penetration broadly across the Architectural Specialties categories. Order intake in the quarter again increased at a double-digit rate, reflecting recent strong bidding activity and providing a healthy foundation as we finish 2026 and move into 2027. Adjusted EBITDA in this segment increased 25%, and adjusted EBITDA margin expanded 60 basis points to 19.4%.

That's a strong result given the rapid acceleration of freight costs during the quarter, together with heightened aluminum and steel inflation and tariffs affecting portions of the AS portfolio. As a reminder, particularly with respect to our custom Architectural Specialties work, project pricing remains in place until quotes expire or price escalators take effect. This can create a mitigation lag when inflation and input cost pressures accelerate rapidly. Across the AS portfolio, as we have done across the enterprise, disciplined mitigation measures are in place to reflect the new cost environment, including pricing and cost control actions initiated earlier this year. These efforts help to support the record organic growth and margin expansion achieved this quarter.

This performance gives us even greater confidence in the segment's earnings potential as our actions become more fully reflected in the economics of recently quoted and future projects. Recent acquisitions also contributed to revenue growth in the quarter. We remain focused on accelerating commercial adoption, realizing integration benefits, and using the Armstrong platform to help these businesses scale. The strategic rationale remains compelling. Each portfolio addition expands the range of materials and capabilities we can bring to a project, increases the number of specifications we can pursue, and allows us to capture more value by solving a broader set of customer needs.

We continue to see a clear path toward our 20% Architectural Specialties adjusted EBITDA margin target as we further integrate and scale these recent acquisitions. The performance of Architectural Specialties this quarter also illustrates the broader evolution underway at Armstrong. We are increasingly bringing Mineral Fiber, metal, wood, felt, structural systems, and design capabilities together to help architects, designers, and contractors execute more complex projects. That breadth matters. It allows us to engage earlier, compete for more specifications, and offer customers a more integrated solution from design through installation. Especially in a market where complexity, schedule certainty, and execution matter, our combination of portfolio breadth, technical expertise, project management, and service is increasingly differentiated within our industry.

Our recently completed work at Seattle Tacoma International Airport's Terminal C showcases our expanding ability to bring complex, high-design spaces to life. Working hand-in-hand with the architects over several years, our design team helped realize a striking timber-clad canopy and column structure inspired by Pacific Northwest forests, anchoring a four-story marketplace and grand staircase. The tree at Terminal C, which is pictured on the cover page of our earnings presentation today, combines four distinct wood products with metal ceiling and Mineral Fiber solutions and demonstrates how our broad portfolio and collaborative design expertise uniquely position Armstrong to deliver on ambitious architectural visions. This is exactly the kind of opportunity our expanded portfolio is creating.

We are not simply participating in more categories. We are helping customers solve more of the ceiling and wall scope through one trusted partner. With that, I will turn the call over to Chris for a closer look at our financial results and updated full-year outlook.

Chris Calzaretta: Thanks, Mark, and good morning to everyone on the call. As a reminder throughout my remarks, I will be referring to the slides available on our website, and please note that slide three details our basis of presentation. We begin on slide six with our Mineral Fiber segment results for the third quarter. Mineral Fiber net sales increased 7% in the quarter, driven by AUV growth of 6% and an increase in volumes. The increase in AUV was primarily driven by favorable like-for-like pricing, along with a positive contribution from mix. The increase in volumes was driven by timing-related demand from our Canadian customers, as Mark discussed, as well as benefits from our growth initiatives.

Mineral Fiber segment adjusted EBITDA grew by 8% in the quarter, with adjusted EBITDA margin expanding 40 basis points to 44%. Adjusted EBITDA growth was primarily driven by the fall-through of AUV, although at a lower rate than expected due to the mixed impact of higher sales volumes to lower AUV channels. Additional items contributing to Mineral Fiber adjusted EBITDA growth in the quarter included a solid contribution from our WAVE joint venture, as well as lower Mineral Fiber SG&A expenses, primarily driven by higher incentive compensation expense in the prior year quarter. These benefits were partially offset by higher input costs, reflecting a sequential acceleration in freight and raw material inflation.

While our pricing actions are designed to offset inflation over time, periods of rapid inflation can create timing lags between higher input costs and our full price realization, and this dynamic was a factor during the quarter. Despite elevated input cost inflation, the Mineral Fiber adjusted EBITDA margin of 44% represents the strongest third quarter adjusted EBITDA margin result in the segment since 2019. This level of financial performance, even in a challenging environment, underscores our Mineral Fiber value creation drivers, including AUV growth, annual productivity gains, and positive contributions from our WAVE joint venture, along with our disciplined focus on cost control.

On slide seven, we discuss our Architectural Specialties, or AS, segment results, where we highlight net sales growth of 22%. This increase was driven by broad-based organic growth across most of our specialty product categories, along with contributions from the February acquisition of Eventscape and the 2025 acquisitions of Parallel and Geometrik. We are pleased with an organic growth rate of 16% in the quarter, which represents the highest quarterly organic growth rate on record. AS segment adjusted EBITDA increased 25% versus the prior year, with adjusted EBITDA margin expanding 60 basis points to 19.4%, which includes the dilutive impact of our recent acquisitions.

On an organic basis, we achieved a greater than 20% adjusted EBITDA margin, despite the negative impact of lower project margins related to elevated metal costs and freight. The improvement in adjusted EBITDA was primarily driven by a $12 million benefit from higher net sales, of which $3 million was inorganic. This benefit was partially offset by a $2 million increase in manufacturing costs due primarily to our recent acquisitions, and a $3 million increase in SG&A expenses, of which approximately half was from our recent acquisitions. We are particularly pleased with the segment's $12 million sales benefit to adjusted EBITDA in the quarter, despite the impact of elevated metal costs.

On slide eight, we highlight our third quarter consolidated company metrics. Net sales grew 12% and adjusted EBITDA increased 11%. Additionally, adjusted diluted net earnings per share grew 10%. Incremental volume from both segments, solid AUV performance, and positive equity earnings from WAVE drove adjusted EBITDA growth in the quarter. These benefits were partially offset by higher input costs, primarily driven by freight and raw material inflation, in addition to higher manufacturing costs, which were largely driven by our recent acquisitions. SG&A expenses were essentially flat in the quarter as increased organic selling investments and an inorganic increase from our recent acquisitions were offset by higher incentive compensation expense in the prior year quarter.

Excluding the impact of our recent acquisitions, total company organic adjusted EBITDA margin was 35.1%, representing 40 basis points of organic adjusted EBITDA margin expansion. Slide nine summarizes our year-to-date consolidated company results, which reflect 10% net sales growth and 7% adjusted EBITDA growth. Adjusted EBITDA growth in the first nine months of 2026 was driven by both incremental volume and the fall-through impact of solid Mineral Fiber AUV, as well as positive WAVE equity earnings. Higher manufacturing costs were largely driven by our recent acquisitions and first-quarter costs in the AS organic business. The increase in input costs was driven primarily by freight and raw material inflation.

SG&A increased primarily due to continued investments to support growth, including higher people costs and the inorganic impact from recent acquisitions, partially offset by higher incentive compensation costs in the prior year period. Adjusted diluted net earnings per share grew 9%, driven primarily by higher net earnings and partially due to a lower share count resulting from increased share repurchases. On slide 10, we present our year-to-date adjusted free cash flow performance versus the prior year.

The 2% decrease was driven primarily by unfavorable timing-related working capital flows driven by accounts receivable, higher income tax payments in the current quarter, primarily due to the favorable timing of a portion of our third quarter federal tax payment in the prior year period, and higher capital expenditures. Partially offsetting these headwinds was a year-to-date increase in dividends from our WAVE joint venture. We remain confident in our ability to deliver solid double-digit adjusted free cash flow growth in 2026 to support all of our capital allocation priorities.

Turning to capital deployment, our year-to-date activity reflects continued execution on our capital allocation priorities, which are investing back into the business through CapEx, pursuing strategic acquisitions like the first quarter acquisition of Eventscape, and returning value to our shareholders through our dividend and share repurchases. Last week, we announced a 10% increase to our quarterly dividend, marking the eighth consecutive annual increase since the inception of our dividend program in 2018. This increase reflects our board of directors' continued confidence in our growth strategy and ability to generate adjusted free cash flow growth.

Additionally, in the third quarter, we paid $15 million of dividends to our shareholders and repurchased $75 million of shares, bringing our year-to-date dividends paid to $45 million and our year-to-date share repurchases to $210 million. As of September 30, 2026, we have over $1 billion remaining under the existing share repurchase authorization. Our balance sheet is healthy. With low leverage and ample available liquidity, we remain well positioned to execute on our strategy. Turning to slide 11, we present our updated 2026 guidance metrics. We now expect total company net sales growth of 10%-11%, up slightly from the previous range of 9%-11%.

This increase is driven by the continued robust performance of our AS organic business, where we now expect net sales growth in the low teens range, up from our previous assumption of growth in the high single digits. Partially offsetting this increase are lower than expected contributions from our recent acquisitions as we continue to scale these businesses onto the Armstrong platform. This brings our full year net sales outlook for the AS segment up to 18%-19% growth, an increase from our previous outlook of 15%-17% growth. For the Mineral Fiber segment, we have slightly lowered our full year net sales growth assumption to approximately 6.5%, with about one point of volume growth and AUV growth of approximately 5.5%.

We now expect more price due to higher inflation and softer mix versus our prior expectations. Additionally, we are modestly lowering the midpoint of our total company adjusted EBITDA guidance and now expect growth of 8%-10% for the full year, down from our prior guide of 9%-12%. This adjustment incorporates elevated input cost inflation and the ongoing impact of tariffs and reflects the timing of our mitigation actions across the business, as well as lower than expected contributions from our recent acquisitions in AS. In the Mineral Fiber segment, we now expect the full year adjusted EBITDA margin result to be approximately flat to the prior year, largely reflecting the impact of mixed headwinds.

Recall that in Mineral Fiber, the 2025 adjusted EBITDA margin result of 43.5% was a record full-year result, surpassing the previous high watermark of 2019. Within the AS segment, we continue to expect full year adjusted EBITDA margin expansion with an adjusted EBITDA margin of approximately 19%. And on an organic basis, we continue to expect a full year adjusted EBITDA margin of approximately 20%. We have also slightly lowered the midpoints of both our total company adjusted diluted net earnings per share and adjusted free cash flow guidance, given the decrease in our total company adjusted EBITDA guidance.

We now expect total adjusted diluted net earnings per share growth in the range of 12%-13%, down from our prior guide of 12%-15%, and now expect adjusted free cash flow growth of 10%-13% compared to our prior guide of 10%-14%. Please note that additional assumptions are available in the appendix of this presentation. Our updated full year guidance reflects our current view of the business, and we remain well positioned to deliver another year of profitable top-line growth. Now I'll turn it over to Mark for further commentary.

Mark Hershey: Thanks, Chris. The outlook Chris just summarized reflects the market conditions and on-the-ground sentiment we are seeing today. Broadly, underlying market conditions remain generally muted, consistent with what we described last quarter and have experienced more or less throughout 2026. As a reminder, we benefit from a broad mix of end markets and balanced exposure across new construction, renovation, and repair and replacement activity, providing resilience across changing market conditions such as those we are seeing today. We continue to see steady activity across a diverse range of verticals with strength in areas such as transportation, healthcare, and data centers.

Within those verticals, we are encouraged by the trend we are seeing toward high-value, experiential infrastructure for airports, stadiums, arenas, and other hospitality environments. Ongoing modernization, capacity investments, and a greater emphasis on the passenger and fan experience are driving more architecturally differentiated premium spaces. This plays well across both Mineral Fiber and Architectural Specialties. Importantly, that can mean not just more square footage, but greater design intensity per square foot with more emphasis on acoustics, aesthetics, and differentiated solutions. At the same time, we are closely monitoring elevated inflation, geopolitical uncertainty, and signs of more cautious sentiment across the industry.

While on-the-ground sentiment and bidding activity remain stable, we are working closely with our customers, particularly our commercial distribution partners, to monitor for signs of project delays and capture opportunities as they emerge. In this type of environment, our strong relationships with distribution partners, architects, and contractors, together with continued investment in our growth platforms, remain critical to expanding our addressable market and driving growth ahead of the market. Within our growth initiatives, our e-commerce platform, Kanopi, continues to demonstrate solid growth with double-digit revenue and Mineral Fiber volume growth year to date and meaningful EBITDA improvement, while increasingly extending its digital capabilities beyond Mineral Fiber into grid and Architectural Specialties.

Similarly, ProjectWorks, our automated design platform, also continues to grow, adding incremental Mineral Fiber volume growth while also assisting designers and contractors in the design and pre-construction project phases, helping save time and money with complete, accurate, and dependable solutions, drawings, and bills of the material. In data centers, we are applying Armstrong's established strengths in specification, systems, technical support, and installation efficiency to an attractive growing vertical. As we shared last quarter, our broader solution set has positioned us to serve demand from hyperscalers, co-locators, and enterprise customers more effectively. Our complete set of structural grid, containment, and integrated Mineral Fiber solutions can improve building efficiency and installation speed, and we're seeing encouraging signs of progress.

Through the third quarter, both our ceiling and DYNAMAX structural grid data center sales have exceeded our full-year revenue plans. We are also seeing increased applications for these solutions in mission-critical, advanced manufacturing environments. A recent win at an advanced manufacturing research facility shows how our expanding go-to-market approach and our technical expertise are improving our access and win rate. By engaging earlier in the design and bid process, our close collaboration directly with the owner helped realign project requirements to use our recently introduced SKYLO walkable ceiling system. By validating performance with an on-site demonstration, we turned a specification gap into a confirmed order for our solution.

That project spans almost 500,000 square feet and represents a multimillion-dollar order, demonstrating our ability to win large, complex projects with our tailored solutions and approach. We will use this and other recent wins to strengthen Armstrong's position as a trusted, experienced partner and build references for similar customers. This is a highly competitive market, and our presence continues to expand. But a combination of strong market growth across this vertical, a broader portfolio, and growing customer engagement gives us confidence that data centers and other mission-critical buildings can become a consistent growth platform for us over time. We are also encouraged by the progress we are making with TEMPLOK energy-saving ceilings, particularly in specification activity.

Building on the pipeline growth we discussed last quarter, our specification teams and established sales relationships are helping us introduce TEMPLOK earlier in the design process and earn consideration on a growing range of projects. That's an important step in building demand for a new solution whose value proposition includes energy performance, occupant comfort, and building economics. Specification activity is an encouraging measure of progress. It reinforces our confidence that Armstrong's long-standing strengths with architects, designers, and contractors can help us build this new market by expanding the pipeline of projects in which TEMPLOK can be evaluated, selected, and ultimately converted into commercial demand. We're also seeing compelling real-world validations of our TEMPLOK technology.

A recent case study at a pediatric therapy facility in Charlotte demonstrates how TEMPLOK ceilings are creating a quieter, cooler environment for the children and families the facility serves. This is exactly what excites us about TEMPLOK. We're taking a familiar building surface and giving it an entirely new performance capability, creating tangible value for building owners and occupants. Overall, we remain encouraged by our growth initiatives. They are further differentiating Armstrong with customers, expanding the markets we can serve, and contributing tangible growth today, including up to 150 basis points of above-market Mineral Fiber volume growth for the full year.

In closing, this was a quarter that demonstrated the durability of the Armstrong value creation model and the progress we are making against our growth strategy. We delivered record third quarter sales and margin expansion in both segments, even as input costs accelerated rapidly. We responded with urgency and discipline, and we are confident in the actions underway to address those pressures. At the same time, we are strengthening our established businesses, expanding the Armstrong portfolio, and building growth platforms that increase the value we can deliver for customers. None of that happens without the commitment and hard work of our people.

I want to thank our teams across Armstrong for the way they continue to serve our customers and respond to changing conditions. We move forward into the fourth quarter and look ahead to 2027 with confidence, focused on delivering profitable growth and creating long-term value for our shareholders. With that, operator, we are ready to take your questions.

They could be design capabilities, they could be manufacturing capabilities, or to your point, they could allow us to access the market incrementally through a different channel and go-to-market capabilities. So we look at all of those fronts in terms of the demand and the customers that we're trying to reach. But no real pivot there. That's been consistent with how we've thought about M&A for some time.

Yes, on the data center front, we could see additional capital investments. We'll take those as we always do in the normal course. But we're really pleased with the intake and the growth of data centers, and so at the WAVE joint venture level, investing for that growth and capacity, we're doing that today, quite frankly. Some of the investments we've made this past year have positioned us really well to capture this growth. We'll continue to do that as we see that market continue to grow.

Phil Ng: Okay. All right. Thank you for the color.

Operator: Your next question comes from the line of Brian Biros with TRG. Your line is open. Please go ahead.

Brian Biros: Hey, good morning. Thanks for taking my questions. I'll try to be quick here. On Architectural Specialties, organic growth, great acceleration, higher than the past few quarters. You've talked about great double-digit order intake for the past few quarters. I know you've mentioned visibility into next year. Can you maybe frame up how much of that growth here is winning maybe a greater number of projects versus winning the same share of projects but having more of your products involved, so you kind of cross-sell the portfolio? It feels like probably a mix of both maybe, but just a reminder of how that's trended as you've enhanced the portfolio offerings in the AS segment would be appreciated.

Mark Hershey: Yeah. Thank you, Brian. We don't track it that way, just to say it. But directionally, I think your point is valid. Especially when, for a number of quarters now, we look at Dodge Data, for example, when project count numbers are down, but project values and bidding values are up. I think there's something to your point, and I think that reinforces our strategy, quite frankly. We are winning more on every job, and that's absolutely what we're trying to do. I don't think that's outsized or disproportionate in any way. We've talked to transportation for a number of quarters, which is a vertical that continues to be strong for us.

We're continuing to win at an outsized rate there, I believe. When we win projects like that, we are winning more share of that wallet or that project opportunity. So penetrating a project is absolutely part of the strategy, but I think it's still both, quite frankly. AS is growing at a rate that is obviously, clearly above market growth rates. So we are earning and penetrating categories at, I'd say, at both a project count basis and a total value basis.

Brian Biros: Understood. Appreciate it. Then on just with cost pressures, there's the pricing lag that you probably will eventually get, but there's a time between there. You also have the 3%, roughly, per year, I think, productivity gains you typically point to help offset and drive margins when you can. Can you talk about what actions are currently included in that productivity category, and if there's anything new compared to what you might have been doing a few years ago? Thank you.

Mark Hershey: Yeah. No, Brian, thanks for making the point because I think it's a hallmark of the company and it's a hallmark of the business is the consistency to generate that roughly 3% productivity. We saw that again this quarter. Frankly, the teams sprung into action to try to accelerate some of those productivity actions. You see that in the plants. That's kind of been the hallmark of the business for a really time, where we have operational excellence. We're finding ways to reduce downtime, ways to reduce scrap, really drive our service level performance.

When I talk about a statistic like plant reliability, that directly yields productivity in the plants, because our outputs and our scrap are both optimized when we're performing at that level. So you see it in the manufacturing fabric of this company for a really long time. What we are starting to see is an extension of that offsetting, pushing back inflation to extend more broadly at an enterprise level. It is not just the Mineral Fiber business. The AS operations teams do this very effectively as well, and that mindset extends all the way to all to SG&A. Unequivocally, there is no change in the importance of that value creation driver to the business.

Productivity is very important to us, and it was there right behind the scenes this quarter.

Operator: There are no further questions at this time. I will now turn the call over to Mark Hershey for closing remarks.

Mark Hershey: Well, thank you, everybody, for joining us today and for the questions. We appreciate all of the interest and your support of Armstrong. Have a great day.

Operator: This concludes today's call. Thank you all for joining. You may now disconnect.

#3522. This Blink Outdoor 2K Camera and Doorbell Bundle Is 74% Off Right Now

Pradershika Sharma is a tech deals writer for Lifehacker. She has a Master’s degree in English Literature, a B.Ed., and a TESOL certification. She has been writing professionally since 2018, creating product reviews, affiliate articles, and search ads for global clients while working with Rubix Agency and Cognizant. Previously, she spent a year teaching English at the junior high level.An avid reader since childhood, Pradershika's idea of extreme sports is staying up to read “just one more chapter.” She lives in India.

Amazon has dropped the price of a Blink bundle that pairs the Outdoor 2K+ camera with the Wired Doorbell 2K+ to $38.99 from $149.98—a 74% discount that makes it an affordable way to monitor both your front door and another area around your home, like the driveway or backyard. Blink also includes the Sync Module Core, batteries, and mounting hardware in the package, so there isn't much else you need to get started. Just keep in mind that the doorbell is the wired version, so you'll need existing doorbell wiring to install it. And if you don’t need 2K resolution for the outdoor camera, there’s another bundle for $30.99 that pairs the same 2K video doorbell with the previous generation Blink Outdoor 4, which records in 1080p.

The camera runs on two AA lithium batteries, with Blink estimating up to two years of battery life under typical use and battery-efficient settings—your actual runtime will depend on how often the camera records and the settings you choose. Speaking of, video tops out at 2,560 by 1,440 resolution, and the camera has a 135-degree diagonal field of view, so one unit should cover a typical porch or driveway without requiring particularly precise placement. You also get two-way audio, motion alerts, and night viewing, meaning it covers the basics you would expect from an outdoor security camera.

You should know that some of Blink’s smarter features, including AI-generated descriptions of recorded events, require a paid plan—and cloud clip storage does, too. Plans start at $3.99 per month for one camera, while AI features cost more, so the ongoing expense can add up if you want the full experience. The cameras also fit best into an Alexa-based home because they work with Alexa and IFTTT but not Apple HomeKit or Google Home, notes this PCMag review.

#3521. Amazon’s Kindle Unlimited is now Kindle+, and the 2 big changes include AI audiobooks

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Unlike narrated audiobooks from Audible and other providers, the audiobooks created with Virtual Voice in Kindle+ will instead be narrated by AI.

Amazon just rebranded its Kindle Unlimited subscription to become Kindle+, and will continue to offer unlimited reading from an eligible Kindle catalog for $12 a month. It will also expand its audiobook category to include more titles, including some read by AI narrators.

Kindle+ remains a separate subscription from Prime, and users will still be able to borrow up to 20 titles at once.

The upgrade expands the already available audiobook category through the subscription. It’ll also give authors the option to include more eligible audiobooks in Kindle+, including AI-narrated titles.

Amazon hopes to enhance the reading experience by letting users continue their ebooks with virtual narration as they go about their day after putting down their Kindle. Users will now be able to choose from many more available audiobooks, from traditional narrators to those created with Virtual Voice.

Amazon is offering the Virtual Voice service free to authors in the Kindle Direct Publishing (KDP) program. With Virtual Voice, authors can have their books narrated by AI at no cost, potentially reaching a wider audience.

Also: Got an old Kindle? How to resurrect your e-reader with new books

The Amazon KDP program lets authors self-publish their ebooks on Kindle and enroll in Kindle+ (formerly Kindle Unlimited) to reach more readers. Kindle+-enrolled authors earn money for pages read, not per download. The author’s earnings through Kindle+ depend on the month’s payout rate and the total number of pages read.

As a loyal reader and audiobook listener, I could argue that an AI-narrated book detracts from the human dimension of audiobooks. The service could take jobs away from professional narrators, many of whom have followings of their own.

Also: Why the Paperwhite is still my top Kindle choice

While the audiobook experience differs when created by a narrator versus read by AI, authors enrolled in the KDP program may choose to bypass a human narrator for their audiobooks when a virtual narrator is already available for free.

However, listening to books has been possible on Kindle devices and the Kindle app for years. Some Kindle Unlimited ebooks already included free access to a professionally narrated audiobook, though not all.

Also: You can read PDFs and articles on your Kindle

Kindle accessibility features like Assistive Reader and VoiceView also enabled listening to text read aloud.

There are very few changes to the Kindle+ subscription. Amazon will not increase the price of Kindle+, and users will still be able to borrow books from Kindle+ as they do now with Kindle Unlimited.

Kindle+ is a rebrand, not a new subscription. Readers will get expanded listening options, but the reading experience remains largely unchanged.

Also: Shopping for a Kindle? I recommend this model

You’ll be able to find more audiobooks with your subscription, depending on which authors make them available for Kindle+. Readers looking for exclusively human-narrated and dramatized audiobooks can also use Audible and other platforms, like Libby through their local library, to access them.
